The quiet was refreshing. Since his arrival on Mt. Suzuka, the imps had been grinding away at his shoulder with their ceaseless cries and curses. The sudden silence was refreshing. Only a faint sound echoed in the background. It was something familiar that he hadn’t heard for ages. The sound of a breeze rustling the leaves. And then, he saw a forest and a pasture spread out before him. Suddenly, he felt a presence behind him. He tried to unleash his force to figure out what was going on, but nothing happened. His limbs were numb… he had no power. Curse these human hands.



After making a few observations, Otakemaru soon familiarised himself with the rules of the surroundings. The area where the underwater Mt. Suzuka was located was enveloped in an illusory realm. And since Otakemaru was in the field where Mt. Suzuka was, he had also been dragged into the realm. The realm was more like a picture book, with each picture telling a story. If he failed to shatter the realm he’d been dragged into, Otakemaru had to follow the storyline in the book and play his role to the end. Sometimes he was human, sometimes a spirit.

“I suppose it’s like the play that Suzuka Gozen once performed while she was living with the humans.” Otakemaru thought to himself helplessly while returning to the shore on his boat as a “fisherman”.

The realm seemed to show no malice. Otakemaru’s “story” was sometimes short and sometimes long. And there never seemed to be any connection between his identities. Otakemaru failed to find the key to deciphering the realm at first.

“I never thought I’d see the sea from the shore again.”

Otakemaru tied his boat to a post with mixed feelings as he watched the sun painting the sea red. Then he heard footsteps behind him, and once again felt the dull pain of his soul being torn apart. Otakemaru turned around as he normally would, bidding silent farewell to the sea behind him, and walked past a fisherman who was walking towards the sea. The man was wearing thin clothes and a straw hat, holding an oar in his hand. He looked just like any other fisherman on the shore except that he had a different colour. In fact, he looked quite conspicuous, for he had only one colour, as if someone had crammed a filthy and broken soul into the empty husk of a man and painted him gold. One could easily spot the difference no matter how hard he tried to fake it.

This amused Otakemaru to no end.



Life in the realm continued. Each time the narrator announced the beginning of a new life, Otakemaru felt his power recovering. Having received mentorship from Uminari, he knew a few ways to break the illusory realms. But rather than getting out of the realms, he wanted to know more about them, such as how they appeared out of thin air and broke the peace of his mundane life in the field. He wanted to know why they were there and what they wanted. He had some guesses and was waiting for a chance to verify his theory.

“The young leader struggled to wake up from his dream and start his day.”

This time, he was a human general on his way to inspect a village by the sea. On entering, he heard merchants peddling their wares, villagers chatting, and children playing… All of the sounds would’ve been convincing if not for the fact that all of the villagers had golden bodies and blurred faces. They were the imps who’d been dragged into the realm along with Otakemaru. After experiencing dozens of stories, the imps, who possessed no self-consciousness, stopped crying. Their annoying whispers turned into mumbling. Amongst the golden crowd, Otakemaru closed his eyes and listened to the noisy but pleasing voices. He sighed, thinking that the world in the picture realm looked almost the same as his past life. He also knew clearly that this reality was born of illusion. He opened his eyes and left the village, helping a child who had tripped and fallen on his way out. The child looked up and smiled, or he seemed to, but it was hard to tell because he didn’t have a face. Otakemaru knew it was just a guest who’d accidentally crashed into an illusory realm and had been clumsily experiencing someone else’s joy and sorrow through a series of neatly arranged but random stories.

Otakemaru opened his eyes again and found himself standing on the beach of Mt. Suzuka. The morning sun had painted the sea, the island and the mountain as red as the torii gate. Otakemaru heard a lament, “The Young Master of Mt. Suzuka woke up from his slumber and started his day.”

This time, Otakemaru himself was in the picture. He looked down at his hands to see the scaly born contaminations, as well as many small cuts and bruises. Without hesitating, he conjured Kenmyoren and slashed at the sea in front of him. A wave of demon force cleaved the sea and the sky open, emitting a sound of paper being torn. Burning pieces of paper rained down from the sky onto the small island in the sea. The golden imps stopped and watched the realm as it crumbled with Otakemaru.



Otakemaru opened his eyes in a red world. He was at the bottom of the sea he knew so well. The full moon cast a silver light across the field. Otakemaru felt as if the days he’d spent in the realm had lasted mere moments. He looked around. The wandering imps in the Mt. Suzuka ruins had quietened down. Through the gloom, the red framed screen of a mountain island appeared, painted red by the dawning sun. From its outline Otakemaru recognized his Mt. Suzuka. He understood immediately. He unraveled the screen to reveal a letter. The realm had been created by the screen that came with the letter. Murmuring, Otakemaru tried to figure out how it had been sent and unraveled the letter. He soon understood the whole secret, and sighed helplessly as he gazed once again at the screen and the wandering imps behind it.

The screen had originated from a painting of the old Mt. Suzuka, as Otakemaru had predicted. Suzuka Gozen had come across the screen in a village where the shop owner had claimed it had been created by a traveling painter. The painter had suffered a creative block before he’d decided to embark on a journey. However, he had encountered a violent storm while traveling. When he finally made it through and saw the clear sky after the rain, he discovered an island. Under the clear sky, the sea, the island and the mountain were all painted red by the dawning sun. It was beautiful. Having lost most of his tools in the storm, the painter ripped apart his clothes and bit his finger, using the blood to complete a rough sketch on the rocky ship. He locked himself away to finish the painting, which ultimately became the screen.

Suzuka Gozen felt an affinity with the painting that depicted her home, so she’d bought it and sent it to Otakemaru. Worrying that a screen alone might be too boring and Otakemaru might not have a place to put it in his underwater field, she asked her friends in Heian-Kyo to make the screen “special” so her brother could “enjoy” it, even at the bottom of the sea. Reading the letter, hearing Suzuka Gozen’s pride in every word, he thought back on his experiences in the realm. Otakemaru did not thank those kind friends for their “favors.”

According to Suzuka Gozen, she had only asked her friends to infuse the screen with spiritual power so it would look more vivid. But the spirit in the painting who had been looking for inspiration, had turned the screen into a world before her story-lover friend had known about it. The friend gave countless “possibilities” to the painting realm after getting inspiration from the legends of a certain mountain god. She created countless stories in the small world with endings that the author herself didn’t know. Then Suzuka Gozen came across a girl who loved theatre and masks. She wanted to “help” too. Worrying that the recipient of the gift might feel lonely while enjoying the painting, she gave proper characters to the figures in the painting. So, with all this “help” from those kind ladies, an ordinary screen was turned into an artifact capable of pulling people in to experience a myriad of stories.

At the end of the letter, Suzuka Gozen mentioned some turmoil in the capital that had prevented her from experiencing the screen herself. She didn’t know when the letter would be delivered and hoped the screen would remind him of her, to help with his pain.

The pain caused by the haunting imps…

Otakemaru put the letter away and looked back at the wandering imps in the field. The imps, which had been contaminated by the miasma which had accidentally been dragged into the realm, were perhaps just like him. They had memories of their experiences in the realm, which seemed to have granted them consciousness. The underwater field was quiet for the first time. Thinking back on the golden figures who were clumsily dragging their bodies along in the realm and imitating the actions of ordinary people, Otakemaru looked at the imps who were now wandering the crumbled ruins and reached out his hand to draw an outline of Mt. Suzuka along the lines on the screen. He was not the only one missing his home, wanting a peaceful life. Otakemaru approached the field wall and sent a message into the distance through the current.


This was the Talisman Pass event for November. The focus of the event was SP Ubume.

The limited rewards for the November Talisman Pass were the Scarlet Scent skin, Making the Gift and Message Sent loading screen illustrations, the still version of the Scarlet Feather icon frame and the animated version of the Dark Scarlet Feather icon frame.


In the morning sunshine, birds chirped and a morning breeze swept the desk, carrying the first chill of fall. A thin strand of incense smoke dissipated in the air. A fragment of a letter fluttered through the air. Just as it was about to land on the ground, a bird appeared and fetched it back to the desk, before it dissipated into black smoke, leaving two scarlet feathers in its wake. Surprisingly, the two feathers were heavy enough to keep the thick letter paper on the desk. Several children were playing in the distance. The owner of the house had not yet returned from her long journey, but the ever-burning incense and the fall breeze were taking care of her letters on the desk. The letter on the top of the stack seemed to have been folded and unfolded many times, leaving deep creases in the yellowed paper.

“How are you, Master? Please accept my apologies for not bidding you farewell. We used to send Mayoi and other seniors off together. Now it’s my time to walk the same path heading down the mountain. Unlike them, I don’t have the courage to bid you farewell in person. So I snuck out at night and sent you this letter. It’s not that I don’t want to say goodbye in person, I just wasn’t brave enough. And I knew you would ask me to practice my swordsmanship more if you saw how weak I was. But I know you’d never stop us from doing the things we’ve set our hearts on.”

“We’ve been living on the mountain these past few years, but rarely had the chance to go down the mountain. The last time I saw what I’m seeing now was the time I barged into the village. I still feel sorry for what I did, even now. I was unhappy about my family’s rules and how my parents had decided to educate me, so I left home knowing nothing about anything. I was lucky to barge into the village and meet you before I ran into any monsters or bad guys.”

“I must’ve been quite a headache for you when I was little, behaving like a spoilt, stuck-up brat. But of course, you educated and punished me accordingly. And thanks to your education, I have been changed for the better. Remember once when I said I’d had enough? I threw away my wooden blade and had a real tantrum. I said I was different from the peasants and the spirits here, and wanted to go home, forgetting that I was the one who’d volunteered to come in the first place. You looked at me in silence and pointed to the other kids in the distance. You said, ‘Of course you’re different. You still have a place to go to, while they no longer have a home they can return to.’ After hearing your words, I cried. When I was little, I thought I was the center of the world. I didn’t know I was only a small part of it until that moment. Without fully understanding the world, I could only cry. Perhaps, that’s what they call growing pains.”

“Now that I’ve completed my practice, I’ve decided to see the world with my own eyes. I wanted to understand why you created this paradise for us. What made you who you are. Your strict mentorship will always guide me.”

“Attached please find the incense I bought from the shop at the foot of the mountain. I remember Mayoi used to love this smell. I wish you all the best.”



A traveller with an umbrella entered a small town. It was a long way from Heian-kyo, but just as lively. Peculiarly, there were not only humans in the streets, but also spirits who appeared to live their life just like the humans. So the traveller didn’t get any attention as she walked along, holding her umbrella with her scarlet feathered hand; the townsfolk saw her as an ordinary person.

She took a letter from her sleeve. The paper was yellowed and worn, and the writing was blurred, as if it had undertaken a long journey before it had been received. The spirit with the umbrella scanned the street, letter in hand, trying to find a house which was described in the letter. After a short walk, she stopped at a small pawnbroker’s shop. 

The shop owner greeted his customer lazily after waking up from a nap. The customer nodded as she politely listened to the shop owner introducing his products. Time seemed to have stopped in the shop. Everything in the shop was in its original packaging. She noticed a painting depicting a mountain forest, small houses, a waterfall, and children playing in the woods… it wasn’t a particularly good painting, just ordinary. In a blank space on the painting, there was some writing.

“The children are playing and the stoves have been lit. The birds have returned to the woods.”

“The journey is long and the roads are winding. The sun sets on another mountain.”

Just when the shop owner was about to ask the customer if she needed any help after seeing her interest in the painting, the customer showed him the folded letter. She asked him how much she should be charged for putting the letter on deposit. The shop owner took the letter and put it next to the painting without giving her a receipt. 

“Don’t worry. A letter doesn’t take up much space. I’ll just put it next to the painting.”

The customer with scarlet feathers thanked the shop owner. Before she disappeared into the bustling street, the shop owner, unable to restrain his curiosity, asked,

“Do you know the owner of the painting?”

There were a few lines visible on the half-opened letter on the desk. 

“...I miss life in the village the more time I spend on the road. After experiencing the chaotic nature of life, I am beginning to understand your kindness and your thoughtfulness. Life where humans and spirits live together in harmony is very rare in the outside world. It’s a shame I’m no longer a child and I can no longer live an ignorant life under your wings. There are many things I can do in the outside world. I promise to live up to your mentorship.”

The lady with the umbrella turned around and looked at the shop owner as she nodded. 



It’s been ages since anyone set foot on the path to the rear mountain behind the small town. The unbridled weeds have grown tall. The traveler with the umbrella crossed the dewy grassland without getting the slightest bit wet. Wherever she went, birds flew out of the misty clouds. The weeds were cut as she passed by. It didn’t take long to clear a path. But she didn’t stop there. She continued walking until she’d reached the mountaintop.

It was still early morning. The sun shone weakly across the mountaintop. She took a stack of letters from her sleeves and started to read. Some were long and some were short. Some were written on fine silk, and some on rough grass paper. Some were covered in smudges making them hard to read. And yet, she read every one attentively, trying to find the letter of the peremptory girl she remembered in every word.

“...as I suspected, Father and Mother see me as a disgrace to the family. They no longer remember having a daughter who waywardly ran away from home. But it no longer matters to me. I’m happy enough to have you and the others as my family members. Still, I felt sad when the servants cast me out of my home. But my situation now is not half as bad as yours was when people complained about you for what you’d done to the village. I will do my best to prove myself.”

“...accept my apologies for the awful handwriting. I was wounded when I tried to save a few travelers from the monsters a few days ago. But don’t worry about me. I’m absolutely fine. I was thrilled to receive your letter a few days ago. I’m sorry to worry you. I wasn’t trying to be secretive. I really didn’t want you to worry. I’m not sure if I would send this letter. After all, you’d definitely worry about me when you read it.”

“...days ago, I ran into Mayoi. She invited me to have dinner in her home. She also introduced me to her family. I was told she and her friends protected the villages from the monster attack years ago. I must learn from her! I’m considering stopping somewhere for a while before I start my next journey. What do you think, Master?”

“I found a boy in the mountain. I believe he’s just like I was once, a boy who’d run away from home. He reminds me of myself… perhaps less obnoxious. Sorry for bringing you so much trouble!”

“...thank you so much for your advice! I’ve been quite busy on the road and totally forgot to send a letter to you. Attached please find the incense I made myself. May it bring you peace and good luck.”

“Please forgive my handwriting - I’m writing to you during my break. Somehow I have embarked on the same path that my mother and father once walked. But whenever I think that my position in the government could create a peaceful living environment for everyone, just as you created a paradise for humans and spirits, I feel motivated.”

“... the construction of the small town is finally complete. I’m now writing to you under the moonlight at the mountaintop while looking at the houses with lights at the foot of the mountain. I used to feel painful for the limited things I can do. But, right now, looking at the lights at the foot of the mountain, I slowly understand that compared with the things I failed, the ones I protected and the things I created are more precious. Now I fully understand your thoughtfulness. I hope I live up to your expectations. Your tough mentoring will always guide me.”

Just when the traveler with an umbrella finished her last letter, she heard footsteps coming from behind.



The lady who approached was holding a bucket of water. Confused, she stared at the spirit with the umbrella.

“You can call me Ubume.” Turning her head, the spirit with scarlet feathers smiled sweetly to show friendliness.

“Oh, you’re that…!” The lady covered her mouth in surprise. With a shy smile, she quickly explained. “My father told me about you. And… he must’ve heard it from her mother, my grandma.” Curious, she stepped closer, trying to get a better look at the spirit.

“I used to come here with my father, but he’s old and his legs are weak. Now I’m always busy with the shop, so we don’t have the time to come here very often. I planned to clear the weeds on the mountain path only to find they’ve already been cleared. That must be your work! Thank you so much!”

“Your grandma…” Ubume thought for a moment and smiled as she turned to the dawning sun. “How do you remember her?”

The lady thought for a moment and said, “She was a very capable woman, a great lady! You must’ve already been through the village since you’ve come to this mountain at the end of the range. It’s all thanks to her that our town is how it is now! When I was little, my father told me how she and her friends met, and how they defended the village against the monsters, so that now it has become this prosperous town. As far as I can remember, she was a decisive woman. Nothing could sway her, once she’d made up her mind about something. I don’t suppose… she received mentorship from you, did she?”

The lady bent down, taking out a soft cloth from the bucket, and gave the tombstone in front of her a wipe.

“She was so determined that even when she was old, she still refused to rest and always wanted to do things for others. Have you come to visit her too?”

Guardian Ubume nodded and knelt down by the lady and they both gazed at the tombstone.

It was as if decades had passed in just seconds. Guardian Ubume looked at the stone tablet washed by time and saw the girl she remembered from so many years ago. She was throwing a tantrum in the village, running around and knocking everything down. Guardian Ubume closed her eyes and remembered every letter she had read. She could picture how the girl had written to her. Those thin pieces of paper recorded her whole life.

“The lifespan of spirits is far longer than that of humans. It’s even more so for tutors like myself. I… seem to have always been bidding farewell to my children.”

Hearing her sigh, the lady turned and looked at the spirit who looked the same age as her.

“But for every one of your students, your mentorship was enough to make their life complete. You are their memories and you will provide guidance for many more people in the future.”

Under the dawning sun, Ubume lit an incense in front of the tombstone. As many years had passed since the incense had been made, it had gotten damp and difficult to light. The technique used to craft it had been poor, as the stick was not even straight. But when the old incense was lit and the smoke slowly rose up under the dawning sun, birds spanned their wings and flew out from the misty clouds, each with a letter in their beaks as they reached for the unreachable sky.


This was the Talisman Pass for December. The focus of the event was Hatsurei Yamakaze.

The limited rewards for the December Talisman Pass event was the Silent Wind skin, Howling Wind frame, Wuthering Wind Deluxe frame, Forest Strike illustration, and the Dust Settled illustration.


“Do you have any customs where you come from, Yamakaze?”

Yamakaze was leaning against a tree and polishing his blades under the setting sun when Kaoru asked him this question. Thanks to the coming of winter and the Six Realms Gates’ influence on Heian-Kyo and the suburbs, nightfall was coming earlier and earlier. Shorter daylight hours are bad news for the folks living in the forest—especially children, who have less time to play. Kaoru idly sat by Owl as she talked about the things she’d heard and seen from the human children she’d played with that day. They’d told her about the things they were preparing for the end of a year and the coming of the new year. Then she turned to badger Yamakaze again.

“Customs? We don’t have such things.”

“Every village has customs.” Kaoru waved at Ichimokuren and his follower as they returned from their patrol in the mountain, and continued, “Master Ichimokuren once told me that different living environments will give birth to different customs. You must have customs that are different from Mt. Septem’s where you come from. I want to know. Please?”

Shishio approached them after returning from the patrol with Ichimokuren.

“You must come from a distant land just like me, Master Yamakaze. Please tell us about it!” Shishio and Kaoru looked expectantly at Yamakaze, who was wiping his blades. He put them away and looked at Ichimokuren, as if he was asking for help. The gentle Wind God smiled silently, as if he too was waiting for Yamakaze’s answer.

“Tsk,” Yamakaze thought for a second and then said, “The place I come from was much more dangerous than Mt. Septem and the surrounding areas. Our customs were just what we had to do to stay alive. Nothing worth noting, really.”

Kaoru lowered her head in disappointment. Yamakaze continued, “We’d bury the things we loved in the woods… if you see that as a custom.”

“I’ve heard about similar customs! You bury them so you can dig them up again many years later, right?”

“Not really. We buried them but never dug them up.”

“But why? If it’s something you cherished, aren’t you supposed to carry it with you? Wouldn’t it be lost if you buried it in the woods?”

Yamakaze remained silent for a while, as if thinking, before he continued, “We believe that, when a life leaves us, it returns to the forest and becomes one with it. As we’re never sure if we will live long enough to retrieve those things, it is more reassuring to keep them in the forest.”

“We’re going to bury something. Are you coming?”

Matsukaze asked Valiant Yamakaze as he stumbled upon his friends.

The children in the village had picked up a special custom recently. They would go and find a place outside the village to bury their favourite weapons, their best accessories, or their most cherished pieces of loot in the mud. It would apparently make one fight more fiercely in future battles.

But wasn’t it no different from throwing these things away, burning them under the weeds? Valiant Yamakaze was not interested in their so-called “custom”. There could not be any connection between burying something underground and becoming more powerful, he thought. But his friends were crazy about this. They’d often run away mysteriously after hunting and return with great anticipation and happy smiles on their faces.

“I’m not interested.”

Valiant Yamakaze ignored Matsukaze and continued on his way out of the village, to check the traps he had laid a few days earlier. Matsukaze smiled bitterly as he took out his weapons, ready to accompany him.

“I really envy you, you know. You’re always so simple-minded.” Matsukaze explained after seeing his friend staring at him in disbelief. “I’m not mocking you! I understand why everyone does this. After all, unlike you, it’s hard for us to focus ourselves in battles without a little superstition.”

“Why? Just don’t think about anything else.”

Matsukaze was surprised by Valiant Yamakaze’s response. But then he sighed.

“We can’t. Life is full of ‘anything else’. And it’s best we leave these things behind while hunting. Otherwise we’ll be slowed down, unable to maintain a fearless outlook. And you collect trophies, too. They just don’t affect you because you’re more powerful than us.”

Matsukaze pointed at the wolf’s tooth accessories dangling from Valiant Yamakaze’s outfit.

“And how’s burying them underground working out for you?”

“They’d feel reassured that if we died someday, we’d leave the world with the things we cherished and become one together with the forest.”

“They?” Valiant Yamakaze raised an eyebrow and looked at his friend. “What about you?”

“Well, I’d probably…”

The answer was lost to time. Yamakaze stopped reminiscing about the past and looked at his friends.

“Anyway, we had this custom.”

He tried to end the conversation. But Kaoru grew more curious.

“What about you, Yamakaze? Did you bury anything?”

“Did you bury anything in the forest?” Valiant Yamakaze asked Matsukaze, who was standing next to him in silence.

They were standing in the forest outside the village staring at an inconspicuous pile of dirt. Not long ago, one of their friends had buried something he cherished there, hoping that the treasures in the woods would bring him luck and victory. They could still make out his traces on the pile. However, he would never bury anything ever again. Matsukaze buried the remains he had picked up from the battlefield in the pile. Seeing that it didn’t look natural enough, he put more dirt on it, only to make it look even stranger. He stopped and stared at the ground in silence. Valiant Yamakaze eventually grew bored and started a conversation. Matsukaze stood up and answered, “Yeah, but nothing good.”

Valiant Yamakaze gestured that they should return to the leader in preparation for the next hunt. Matsukaze waved his hand at the pile of dirt in the open space and left.

“It’s not that I believe burying things could make me more powerful. I just want to leave something for myself.”

“Something for yourself?”

“Whenever I’m afraid, I remind myself that there’s something buried underground waiting for me. Think of it as a gift.”

“I thought you’re not supposed to take them out after burying them.”

“Well, that’s not something a headstrong brute like you would understand.”

Briskey landed on Valiant Yamakaze’s shoulder and started cooing while fluttering its wings.

“See? Briskey agrees with me.”

Matsukaze tried to tap Valiant Yamakaze’s head, but was knocked away by a flap of Briskey’s wings. He smiled bitterly while rubbing his hand.

“I just hope we can live a peaceful life under the leader’s guidance. When that day comes, and I’m no longer here, you can dig my things up if you’re curious.”

“That won’t be necessary.”

“Okay, I know you’re not interested. But in the future…”

The expectation for the future was worn away by the growing danger in their living environment. The battle against the wolf spirits grew fiercer, and the villagers soon forgot about the “custom” as they were too busy with fending off their enemies. The small piles of dirt with their buried treasures were slowly covered by weeds. After a fire was lit when the wolf spirits launched their final attack, the buried belongings became one with the land, lost in the forest forever.

“So did you go back to see what your friend buried afterwards?” asked Kaoru sadly after hearing the story.

“No. The villagers went on to migrate a few times. The last time I went back, there was nothing but burned ruins.” As the last ray of sunshine disappeared over the horizon, night fell and the forest became silent. Yamakaze sheathed his blades, ready to head home. Seeing that Kaoru still looked sad, he tried to comfort her. “The lives that left us have already returned to the forest’s embrace. There’s no need to feel sad.”

“Let’s go and take a look, shall we, Yamakaze?” Kaoru tried to say more, but stopped after seeing the look on Yamakaze’s face. “Sorry, I just want to…”

Ichimokuren, who had been listening in silence, stepped in and said, “The forest is more dangerous in winter, Kaoru. We don’t know when the turmoil in this area will end. Yamakaze is worried about you.”

Yamakaze looked at his blades nonchalantly without saying anything more.

Since that day, Yamakaze noticed that Kaoru seemed quite enthusiastic about “dragging him home to find the things his friend had buried.” Perhaps it was because his story had such an impact on the little girl. Thankfully, Ichimokuren persuaded her not to do anything dangerous, so Yamakaze allowed her to keep her little secret.

Then one day, Yamakaze had a special dream. The moment he entered the dream, he regained his consciousness. He was fully aware of the fact that he was dreaming, and yet, he was unable to get out. In his dream, Yamakaze had returned to his childhood. His former partner was hovering in the air above him while the wolf spirits were lurking in the shadows in the forest, their eyes flickering with an ominous light. He was heading somewhere but had no control over where he was going. After sensing the dream was not malicious in nature, he stopped struggling. He walked past the place where his village was once located. The burned ruins were now covered in moss. Wild flowers were blooming among piles of dead bones. Marks of the past had been turned to ashes by the wheel of time. He stopped at the mountain top, his destination, and saw a lush tree blooming with flowers on every branch. Yamakaze didn’t recognize the flowers. He didn’t even know if what he was seeing in this dream was reality, or if it was all imaginary. A breeze swept by, and he heard voices coming from the ruins of the village. The voices of his “friends” he reluctantly recognized, the voice of the leader lecturing them, and the voices of other villagers. He woke up before he could turn around.

The next day, Yamakaze asked Kaoru if she had done anything. The girl made a face and ran away. He asked the others on Mt. Septem, though their only responses were coy smiles. Yamakaze stopped chasing everyone for answers and assumed Kaoru and Ichimokuren had asked some spirit to take him back to his home in a dream that could cross reality.

“So, what did you dream of? Did you see your buried friend? Tell me.”

Yamakaze ignored Kaoru and found some higher ground so he could be alone. He looked into the distance. In the lifeless forest, Kaoru was chatting with Mushishi, Ichimokuren was sitting next to Shishio, listening to them, and Koroka was trying to join the conversation. He had received many things from them: Owl’s feathers, wind amulets, wild berries picked in the forest, cocoons for tending to wounds… He suddenly understood his friends’ intentions when they’d buried their cherished possessions. But for Yamakaze now, those things were not gifts prepared for his return to the forest when life had ended, but blessings both meaningful and filled with the hopes and expectations accumulated throughout his lifetime.
This was the Talisman Pass event for January. The focus of the event was Ukiyo Aoandon.

The limited rewards for the January Talisman Pass event were Fool's Crossing skin, Swaying Ship frame, Homing Ship deluxe frame, Unworldly Game illustration, and Across the Sea illustration.


The first snow of the winter fell in the evening and painted Heian-Kyo white. I packed up my things in the snowstorm and set off in the bitter cold. My two servants, wrapped snugly in thick clothing, rubbed their sleepy eyes and walked out of the empty, quiet courtyard with me. They have long been used to traveling with me, having no fixed place to stay, and embarking on strange adventures every day.

“Where are we going this time, Lady Aoandon?”

“A forgotten sea.”

My servants looked puzzled and asked, “Is there… a story waiting for you there, My Lady?”

“Most of the dust has settled in that place. But on the first snowy day every year, I think back to a promise I made at sea. Alas, I’m probably the only one in this world who still remembers it. Anyway, just follow me.”

On the dark and gloomy sea, an abandoned ferry boat was slowly rowing into the distance. I stood on the bow and looked at the already dim capital in the distance. We were drifting on the sea and heading for an unknown ruin. Then suddenly, my servants stopped rowing the boat when a shore vaguely emerged on the sea in the middle of the night.

“My Lady, we’re about to reach the shore. But… it seems to be a ruin.”

“Just stop the boat here.”

I stood silently on the calm sea and looked at the shore shrouded in darkness. It was almost as if time had stood still on that wrecked coastline.

“My Lady, is the one you’re meeting here?” My servants yawned in the heavy fog, seemingly unsure of our journey.

“He… may need some time.”

I started to picture him in my mind. He was a thin boy, who always smiled in his plain clothes. He lived alone in a boat house that drifted with the waves. He would always row his boat to the calm sea at dusk to sleep with the moon in the sea.

Seeing that my servants still looked confused, I thought for a moment and continued, “If you still have the energy, get yourselves together, for I have a story to tell.”

I reorganized my thoughts and raised my head. Everything seemed to return to the distant past many years ago, when the ruins on the shore were still a harbor with sparkling lights. The familiar boat house was docked at the peaceful pier, and the boy was lying on the stern with his head on his hands and excitedly telling me stories of the sea. Back then, I was just like him, living alone. I have traveled to many places, but I chose to stay here in the end. I then realized that even to this day, I still miss that boat, the sea, and those pure, simple days.

“This is the first time I’ve met a spirit who doesn’t eat people and collects stories instead.”

The boat house was rocking with the waves in the wind when a full moon rose up slowly on the misty sea, beginning another night. The boy had just told me of his work at the levee. Seeing that I was still interested, he felt embarrassed.

The village was located in a remote sea area by a steep peak. It was so secluded that even spirits were scarce. The boy’s only impression of monsters and spirits was from the old scrolls. And yet, he wasn’t afraid of me when he first saw me. I saw him talking to the night birds from afar while I was crossing the sea. From the echoes, I realised that he was practising his paddling skills. I laughed and landed before him when I saw his eyes. A pair of pure bright eyes. Ever since I left the Phoenix Forest, I’d travelled to many places and met many people. But never have I ever seen a face so untainted. I purposefully stopped by the village for a few days. The days I spent with the sea were surprisingly interesting thanks to his stories at night. It was then that I knew the boy grew up at sea. His parents started working in the sea while he was little, so he worked at the levee to support his life in the boat house.

“One day, I will board the far-sailing boat.”

“What boat?”

The boy’s eyes flickered as he started talking about the customs of this secluded town. Every year on the first snow night, a ferry boat would set sail for the land of prosperity. His father was once at the helm of the boat.

“I think my parents are waiting for me in the distant sea.”

I frowned at the boy’s calm tone as I looked at the poor little town built where the mountain connected with the sea. There were few natural resources here, and life was likely difficult for them. When the lights started flickering at midnight, the whole town looked like a stage for horror shows. With that thought in mind, the far-sailing boat seemed even more eerie. But the boy was too young to realise the implication behind it. He kept talking about his life on the shore with excitement, telling me that the fishermen would invite him to catch fish together and their wives at the market would offer him food before nightfall. The more he talked, the more excited he was. His life was tough, but he was grateful. I looked at his shabby house and his shabby clothes. He was so poor that he didn’t even want to light an extra oil lamp. He floated alone on his own in the sea with no one to rely on. If the people of the world were so kind, why didn’t anyone offer him a place to stay? I didn’t ask him if he was being kind or was simply stupid. I responded to him with silence and looked away. I sensed something ominous looming over the town by the steep peak.

I never liked getting involved in the troubling daily life of the mortal world, and I hated travelling during the day even more. So it was almost afternoon when I arrived at the town harbour. The setting sun shone above the sea, casting its light on the piers. One stout fisherman was reprimanding his men while paying them less than what they should have earned. The boy was among those men. He then rushed off to the market with fresh fish in his hands and traded them for a happy smile and a few bites of cold food. I looked at the boy’s frail shoulders, knowing that the people of the world didn’t pity the weak. It was a shame that the boy still believed in this meagre kindness.

“What a fool.”

My thoughts then wandered on to the far-sailing boat as my eyes stopped at the twilight sea. My blue flames started dancing. I waved my hand and an illusory realm was projected onto the harbour. A dilapidated boat was docked on the pier. Poor and depressed townsfolk were boarding the boat in single file. Some were old, and others were weak. The boy’s father, who suffered a leg disease, was among them, as well as his illness-stricken mother. They were slowly entering the cabin. I immediately realised that my premonition was correct. The boat had no destination. There was no Land of Gold. Those who boarded the fools’ boat had given up their hope. The only thing awaiting them: exile.

“The first snow is approaching, Lady Aoandon, and I will be the one who stands at the helm this time.”

One night, I heard the boy’s voice. I immediately sensed something strange. I could still see the “fools boarding the boat” in my mind. But somehow I failed to picture the lively boy on the dilapidated boat.

“You’re boarding the boat this time?”

The boy nodded, thinking about the land of hope, and asked, “Can you tell me what your capital looks like, My Lady?”

“The capital… has a different view.” I paused for a moment and continued, “The capital is always lively and bustling with people.”

“Even when it’s snowing?”

“Even when it’s snowing.”

“We have a small town. Whenever it’s snowing, people lock their doors and windows, and everything looks dark.” The boy’s eyes brightened. “How I wish to see how lights are lit in every house on snowing days.”

“Is this all you wish for?”

“I’m not like you, Lady Aoandon.” He smiled, “I feel lonely sometimes living all by myself in the sea. I feel even more lonely on snowy days. If I could see lights warming up every house… I think I would feel much happier.” The boy thought for a moment and said, “And I think I know where this boat is going this time.”

He’s going to the capital? Looking at his smile, I hesitated for a moment and made my offer, “Why don’t you come with me?”

The boy shook his head and started counting the list of people boarding this time. An old lady who was weak and sick and needed warm tea, and a wife carrying a newborn baby who needed caring for at all times. The boy suspected nothing. From the truth projected by the illusory realm, the engulfing waves would eventually capsize the ferry boat. The boat was doomed from the beginning. Everyone boarding the boat was hopeful, not knowing that they had already been judged as useless. I looked at the calm face of the boy again. My heart felt uneasy. I’m not a god, and I can’t save people. But the boy before me shouldn’t have come to this. As my blue flames flickered, I made up my mind.

Just like all the strange tales I have heard, all stories of supposed miracles are, in the end, nothing more than the greediness of men. So on the first night, I cast the ugly face of the stout fisherman into his dream. The fisherman complained about how clumsy the boy was, how cursed his boat was, and how he was never able to catch big fish. On the second night, the town official went around home after home to gather people for the boat. No matter how well he described the land of prosperity, everyone remained silent. In the end, the town official’s eyes stopped at that lonely boat house in the sea. I tried to persuade the boy to change his mind. However, even as the first snow day was fast approaching, he’d just rub his tired eyes and continue charting his route. I thought he needed to know the truth, so on the third night, I sent his dream into a tumbling sea. The wind was howling, and the whole ship was capsized by the giant waves in mere moments. “Lady Aoandon, what do these dreams mean?” Finally, the boy sat on the deck, feeling confused.

“I was told that before a great disaster, the gods would send messages to the mortal men through dreams. Perhaps that is your destiny. Come with me while we can still change it.” I stopped for a moment and continued, “I have found your parents… they’re waiting for you in the capital. I will take you to them.” I waved my hand. In the illusory realm of blue flames, the boy’s parents sat in a small but warm house with the same kind look on their faces. I had grown adept at fabricating these kinds of illusions, so my face remained unchanged as I waited for the boy’s response. And yet, he threw a glance at the warm realm, as if a flame was dancing in his eyes, and turned to look into the dark and silent distance. The boy didn’t give me an answer until the last night before the first snowfall. It was a misty night when the dilapidated boat docked. I looked at it and saw a dark abyss. I hovered above the shabby boat house and expanded my illusory realm. It was the first time I had showcased my spiritual power. I leaned over to the one standing on the stern. In a world where everyone is foolish and insignificant, none can fight against destiny. I’m not a god, but I believed I could change the ending of one boy. I wanted to get that boy out of there. But he just shook his head with a determined look.

“My Lady… Actually, I volunteered to stand at the helm of this boat.”
This was the Talisman Pass for February. The focus of this event was Ibaraki Douji.

The limited time rewards for the February 2023 Talisman Pass were the Searing Performer skin, Dance of the Demon illustration, 温泉决斗 illustration and 狂景垂幕·鬼演 frame.

This was the Talisman Pass for March. The focus of this event was Mujin Yama Usagi.

The limited time rewards for the March Talisman Pass were the Sweetest Dream skin, Candy Reverie frame, Sweet Candy Reverie animated frame, Adventurous Ninja illustration and Colorful Candy illustration.


“Did you bring the paper, Keita? And Shunsuke and Kaori, did you bring the inkstone, the water tray, and the writing brush? Good, now we can write to the master ninja who travels between dreams and reality. I’ll dictate, and Mei will write for us. You can all add what you want to say.”

A Letter of Request to the Ninja from the Dream

Dear Master Ninja, We’re the Sweet Guardian of Candy Reverie. I hope this request letter finds you well because we are facing the biggest crisis of our lives. A master thief appeared, planning to steal all the stars! He’s nimble and elusive, and appears on the tall Icing Hill every night to collect the Colored Candy Stars with his giant net before running away! No one has seen the face of this star stealer, but he carries the Colored Candy Star mark with a unique glimmer. One look would be enough to recognize him! Wait, I don’t think I’ve told you where Candy Reverie is. Every child who has been to the Rokujuan candy shop and tasted Master Mizukawa’s handmade candies knows where Candy Reverie is! When you go to bed while savouring Master Mizukawa’s marvelous candy creation, you’ll discover a paradise of candies in your dream where the houses are made of rock, the tiles are made of nougat, and the mountains are covered with crystal clear candy trees, blooming with colorful candies. When night falls, our stars, the sparkling colored candies, will rise from the golden Syrup Lake which lies at the foot of the soft, white Icing Hill… Alas, ever since the star stealer appeared, we haven’t yet seen such a sight.

Anyway, if you can help us catch the star stealer, Master Ninja, we’re willing to give you our best candies in gratitude.

From the Sweet Guardians

“What do you think? Master Ninja will definitely help us after reading this letter, won’t he? But how can we sand it to the Master Ninja? Woah, something just rushed past us. Was that a frog? And a cauldron? Forget it. The wind just blew away the letter! Quick, Keita, Shunsuke, Kaori, go get the letter! Oh dear, I wonder if Master NInja will see this letter.

I opened my eyes in the sweet air. I could still taste the sweetness of the candy from Rokujuan. I had arrived at Candy Reverie with Mr. Frog, my ninja partner who could also travel between dreams and reality! To protect the childrens’ sweet dreams, I must catch this star stealer. Just as I committed to this goal, a gleaming candy badge appeared on my chest.

“What a beautiful badge! It looks like a shiny coloured candy,” said Mr. Frog. “This must be a reward for the guardians.”

I gently rubbed the symbol when a strange guy walked past me. He was checking up on everything. There was a coloured candy-shaped star mark on his clothes, and he was holding a giant net. All of this matched the profile of the star stealer in the letter of request! So I walked past, acting casual. The moment he looked away from me, I turned and nimbly jumped onto a swaying candy tree. The strange guy vigilantly looked around, but saw nothing but falling icing.

“I think I saw something… I must be hallucinating,” he thought as he continued walking.

I quickly followed him to the Icing Hill after jumping between candy trees and roof tiles. Sparkling stars were slowly rising from the golden Syrup Lake at the summit, emitting a gentle but bright light. The strange guy stopped by the lake and raised his giant net. In an instant, I threw out my shuriken.

“You’re under arrest, courtesy of the dream ninja, Star Stealer!”

I dashed out, trying to catch him, when suddenly, hands reached out of the soft icing and grabbed my ankles.

Oh no, an ambush! I threw a bomb at the icing, and the little critters hiding there fled. Amidst the white flying dust, I saw children scurrying away, each wearing a star mark on their clothes.

Wait, there’s some kind of misunderstanding… Just as I tried to speak, the strange guy who’d guided me there cast a giant net.

“You’re under arrest by the Sweet Guardians, Star Stealer!”

Me? Star Stealer? Hey, I’m not a bad guy! I’m the ninja from the dream who came at your request!

In the chaos, the bomb exploded. The colourful candies were sent flying with a loud bang. They fell on the ground as the children shouted in amazement. Under the colourful candy rain, a figure with a shining mark on their shoulder flashed past in the dark night. The real star stealer had escaped right under our noses!

On my tenth birthday, my father gave me expensive colored candy as a present for the first time. He said he couldn’t provide me with a luxurious life like the nobles in the capital but, as my father, he wanted to give me the best he could. When I lovingly held up one sparkling candy and looked at it in the sunlight, what I saw wasn’t happiness, but rather the figure of my father toiling hard in the workshop, for one small jar of colored candies. He had to watch the boiling pan day and night, stirring the icing while putting honey in the pan until the tiny candy granules turned into sparkling stars. It took a long time and a great deal of practice.

Mother tried to persuade him, “You’re not made of steel. You need to take a few days off.”

But Father said, “One jar of colored candy is worth more than half a dozen roof tiles plus a foot long stick of fine wood. You’ve always wanted to renovate our workshop, haven’t you? And now, we’re one step closer to our goal.”

I put the candy jar back on the shelf as a thought lingered in my mind: to enjoy these candies would feel like a sin while Mother was worrying so much and Father had to work so hard. I often cried myself when I thought about how hard life could be—living near all this candy but being unable to taste any. But if eating one less jar of colored candy meant that we could add a few roof tiles to our leaky house, I’d rather never know what colored candy tasted like. That night, I went to bed imagining the marvelous taste of colored candy, and a miracle happened. I arrived at Candy Reverie, a world made of candy. Beautiful Colored Candy Stars were sparkling in the sky. Their brilliance moved me to tears. I praised the marvelous miracle! I wanted to offer the Colored Candy Stars to my father! If he could have this many colored candies, he could take some weeks off. And Mother would be closer to her dream of renovating our home. As soon as the thought came to me, a star-shaped mark, bright and blazing like colored candy, appeared on my shoulder. It scorched me as it witnessed a thief with a big bag trapped in the night of Icing Hill. Still, I refused to give up my obsession with colored candies. To me, they symbolized hope, a sweet promise of a prosperous life that we’d never had, just like the sweet taste I had imagined…

Wait, why are the Sweet Guardians crying? Has the justice Ninja Master forgiven me? How strange… I’d felt no regret when they hunted me down, but now, I feel ashamed that they forgive me. I’ll never steal any Colored Candy Stars ever again, and I’ll never return to Candy Reverie, I thought. I wanted to remember their kindness before I woke up.

The next dawn, Jun, the candy workshop owner’s son woke up from his dream and found a special present next to his pillow. It was a strange jar that looked like Frog Ball. Inside were an assortment of candies. He particularly noticed one quirky colored candy that looked like it had an eye. Jun opened the jar and put the candy in his mouth. It was the sweetest taste he’d ever savored.
This was the Talisman Pass for April. The focus of the event was Shinkei Susabi.

The limited time rewards for the April Talisman Pass were Augury Swallow skin, Swallow Augurs frame, Swallow Oracles animated frame, Prophecy illustration and the Swallow Augurs illustration.


As the night fell, everything became silent. The sleeping Heian-Kyo looked as calm as a pool of water. Somewhere in the city, a boy dressed all in black was still awake and busy searching for something in the towering bookshelves, lantern in hand.

“First row, second row, third row…”

The boy counted the bookshelves, without noticing the dim light shimmering somewhere in front of him.

“It’s not here… Huh?”

The boy murmured in confusion, when suddenly, a flash of light from the corner dazed him. After he came to his senses, he realized the light was just a hallucination. But then he saw a handsome young man reading books had appeared in the dark. From the candlelight, he could see that the young man was well-dressed, and that he was immersed in the world of his books, unaware of the stranger who had barged into his place. The boy tried to leave quietly, but he was stopped by a light cough. The sound echoed and amplified in the depths of the library like a stone thrown into a clear lake.

Although the library in Heian-Kyo opens to the public during the day, a heavily garrisoned field will be created at night and will be patrolled by the Ghost Armory of the Minamoto Clan. And the place where the boy had entered belonged to one of the restricted areas where permission to enter was required. The boy vigilantly checked out all movement around him.

“What?”

Just as he was trying to leave, someone stopped him.

“No… nothing. I was just wondering who you are since it’s already this late at night and you’re still reading books.”

The boy regretted the moment he blurted out without thinking. And so, he had no choice but to wait for the man’s response.

“The place is too noisy during the day. The night is quiet and a better time for reading.”

Sensing no malice; the man didn’t seem to be bothered and didn’t even bother to look at him, he calmed down and continued to look for his book. And finally, he found the book he was looking for on a dusty shelf.

“The answer you seek is not in this book.”

The boy was confounded by the man’s words as he tried to read the book by the lantern. He stood there for a moment before he suddenly turned around and took off the black robe he used to cover himself.

“Who are you exactly?”

“Susabi.” The young man who claimed to be Susabi replied without looking at the boy. “Asking the same question over and over again won’t make a particularly good impression.”

The boy thought for a moment. He thought the name seemed familiar, but he couldn’t recall where he’d heard it. The young man in front of him looked the same age as him. His appearance and movements reminded him of an elegant nobleman… or one of the gods whom he’d seen visiting the great onmyoji Seimei’s courtyard not long ago.

“I’m not looking for an answer in the book, but a way to find the answer,” the boy replied. “I’m Kiyoshi, by the way, and I’m an apprentice onmyoji of the Fujiwara Clan.”

Susabi didn’t answer. Kiyoshi suspected that he might not be listening. Still he continued.

“I… um… I dabbled in astrology. A few days ago, I calculated that there will be a meteor passing over Heian-Kyo in three days. But my knowledge in astrology was not enough for me to tell if it was auspicious or ominous.”

“Do you know what this place is?”

“I do…” Kiyoshi replied. “But I’m not here to steal the book. I just want to borrow this book on the stars. Anyway, do you have permission to read here at night, Master Susabi?”

“No, I do not.”

Kiyoshi was shocked to hear such an honest answer, and didn’t know how to respond. But he didn’t give it much thought and started reading the book by the light. The library returned to silence, with only the occasional sound of pages being turned.

“Did you find it?” Susabi suddenly broke the peace after an hour. Too immersed in what he was reading, Kiyoshi was startled by the question and almost dropped the book. He shook his head.

“It’s a rare star… Even the great astrologer didn’t record it in the book…” Kiyoshi seemed frustrated. “It only mentioned that about a thousand years ago, a bright star fell from the sky and disappeared…”

“Why do you care if this falling star is auspicious or ominous?” Susabi closed his book. “What can you do as an apprentice onmyoji even if you have calculated the star?”

Hearing this, Kiyoshi’s eyes dimmed. He pondered for a moment, then slowly closed his book and sighed. He walked to the window and looked at the sparkling stars.

“I… I don’t know. I just think now that I have the opportunity to become an onmyoji, I should take that opportunity.” Kiyoshi thought for a moment and continued, “Thousands and thousands of stars are shining in the boundless sky. As a mortal man… I know how insignificant I am.”

“It’s true that the life of a human is but a grain of sand in the sea to the torrent of time.” Susabi walked to the window unnoticed and put two cups of hot tea on the windowsill before he looked into the boundless sky.

“Everything works in its own way and exists because of destiny. Everything you have been obsessed with, searched for, and sighed about… Someone perhaps had the same experience somewhere in history.” Susabi continued, “That particular someone also broke into the field protecting the library at night a few days ago and made up an excuse to investigate the stars just to find the answers to the Onmyoji Spells test created by the three major clans. Was that you?”

“You… I… That wasn’t me” Kiyoshi denied, blushing, before he hesitantly admitted. “I… something got into me… I just want to check my result…I didn’t try to…”

In the face of Kiyoshi’s defense, Susabi remained silent and continued to look at the sky.

“But… But I didn’t like about the meteor. I may not be an expert in astrology, I’m not mediocre either. I’m sure the star will cross the sky of Heian-Kyo before dawn.”

Before Kiyoshi finished speaking, a bright star flashed across the night sky. Together they silently watched the star fall.

“Most people would make a wish when seeing such a sight. But you didn’t.

“The life of a man has already been predestined. Mortals like us know the beginning. Still, we spend our whole life looking for an ending.” Kiyoshi’s words of sorrow seemed to be answering Susabi, and yet he seemed to be talking to himself.

“Then how do you know the end of one’s life?”

“We will all die eventually. But if we can pry into the future… perhaps, we can clear up some doubts.”

At that moment, the lights on the desk were extinguished. It was dark in the library. But outside the window, the reflections of the dotted stars in the sky seemed to have dimmed in the clear pool. Birds were chirping in the distant forest, telling of the break of dawn. The spring swallows under the eaves awakened. Susabi drawn water from the pool to illuminate the trajectory of the falling star. Kiyoshi stared at the water with admiration in his eyes.

“You’re truly extraordinary.”

“What do you see from that star?”

“The star fell from the north and matches the sign of another star. Auspicious, I assume?” Kiyoshi thought for a moment and gave his answer without hesitation.

“If this is auspicious, did you find what you’re looking for? Have your doubts been cleared up?” Susabi asked.

“Naturally, I…” Kiyoshi smiled and returned the book he borrowed to its rightful place. “I may be an apprentice onmyoji, but I have been learning astrology since childhood through various methods. And because of that, I had a thought of risking my life to alter my destiny with external force… I know it’s a mistake, but I’m willing to keep practicing for a better mastery of my skill to protect this land. I never meant to use it for my own gain” Kiyoshi seemed to be defending himself with righteous words. He looked to the stars and continued. “I will never make the same mistake again.”

The spring swallows circling outside the window seemed to be drawn by the trajectory and started to dance around it, forming a star chart with them being the dotted stars in the sky.

“I beg for your benevolence, Master Susabi…”

As the first ray of morning light broke the dawn, Kiyoshi took a sip of the tea that had gone cold. He tried to beg again when he realized that the man next to him had gone. He turned around and saw a piece of paper on the desk. Something was placed on the paper to prevent the spring breeze from blowing it away. He walked up and saw a line of elegant handwriting.

“Destiny disposes, man proposes.”
This was the Talisman Pass for May. The focus of this event was Jinkougyou.

The limited time rewards for the May Talisman Pass were the Bygone Aroma skin, Wandering Dream frame, Dream Wanderer animated frame, Bygone Dream illustration and the Bygone Butterflies illustration.


“I saw quite a unique thing today. I didn’t believe such a place could exist until I saw that secluded corner where both visitors and animals were enjoying themselves. I wish I had acquired the Letter of Serenity sooner so I could tease… anyone. With that letter, I could hide in the painting scroll after a prank and leave no trace behind. It would be as if I was never there. It’s a shame that I didn’t know about the Letter of Serenity until today. And now, I don’t know where I can use it. Perhaps, when I see Enmusubi again, I could ask her if I can visit that old friend of hers… Then I can use it on him! I’d better note that down and fill in the details when I see her.”

Jinkougyou put down his pen and read his note before closing his notebook with satisfaction. Everyone was curious about what he was writing, as he had been scribbling in his book for some time.

“It took a long time to finish that note, Master Jinkougyou. What is it about?”

The anxious fox Painted Pixie Misty asked first. He’d been circling the desk hoping for an opportunity to approach Jinkougyou since he’d started writing. It was quite obvious he wanted to see what Jinkougyou was writing about.

The mysterious young visitor was a guest of Bukkuman. He once gifted the group some incense he had made. While Bukkuman was roaming around a few days ago, he happened to meet him. Knowing that Jinkougyou was traveling the world, he invited him to see Lady Hana’s new work. Knowledgeable, eloquent, and humorous, he was soon accepted into the group. Even the mischievous Misty found him likeable.

As the new member of the group, he was often the center of attention. And soon, they noticed a little habit of his. He always carried a small notebook and often stopped to scribble, even when they were climbing mountains. The members of the group were all well-educated masters and thus thought it polite to ask. Misty was the first one to break the ice.

Jinkougyou raised his eyebrows and smiled, “It’s nothing special. Just some scribbles recording my journey.”

“So you keep a travel log, my friend Hiro! Could I take a look?”

Bukkuman was intrigued by the topic. He knew about Jinkougyou’s complicated past and that he had something to do with the Six Realms Gates outside the capital. But he only knew rumors and couldn’t reach the gates. Of course he was curious about the travel log.

“You…” Kisei beside him sneered, “But seeing as Bukkuman has already mentioned it… We’re all curious and wonder if…”

“There’s nothing to see, really. Just some random notes that might disappoint you.”

Jinkougyou laughed and handed them his notebook. Bukkuman took it and started skimming through the pages.

“Come on, I’m sure your travel log keeps…”

His voice silenced, as if he had choked on something.

Bukkuman seemed to be inexplicably troubled by something, his face twisting into various expressions.

“What is it? What could possibly make you look like that?”

Maestro, who typically wouldn’t have been interested at all, was suddenly intrigued. He walked up to Bukkuman and threw a glance at the small notebook. Following Bukkuman, he then frowned, revealing an even more perplexed look on his face. That made everyone else curious. They all gathered around Bukkuman to take a look at the new member’s work.

There was nothing wrong with the content… but then again, everything was wrong with the content! At first glance, it looked like a travel log written in Jinkougyou’s usual light tone, talking about the sights and scenes he’d encountered along the way. Some might find it humorous, even. But somehow, these short stories or travel logs, whatever you’d call them, just stopped abruptly at the very peak of the climax. And there would never be any follow-up. It was simply maddening!

“...after the wild boar had become a spirit, it began protecting the forest that granted it shelter. Thanks to nature’s blessing, the forest was abundant with fruits. The inhabitants of the forest lived happy lives, free of hunger and pain. But last month when I travelled past the place, I saw no flourishing forest. There was only dried land and withered trees, and the animals were long gone. According to a farmer living near the forest, there was a monster. It'd be a cruel thing to retell it in detail, so I will leave it be.”

…

“I watched a marvellous game of Go today. Both sides were meticulous, carefully considering where to place each stone. The battles between them were among the most thrilling moments I’d ever seen. So I’m going to record the state of the board for future studies. Then again, such a process might be tedious. I’ll finish it later when I have the time.”

Perhaps it was not meant for Master Jinkougyou to remember those trivial encounters, as none of those notes seemed to lead anywhere. And notes like these, with no beginning or end, accounted for the majority of his notebook. The few complete ones were thoughts he had that others might find completely irrelevant to the subject. Some thoughts were even narrow-minded. But one could laugh at it given how young he still was.

“That's quite a… unique way of writing a travel log…” Bukkuman smiled and shook his head after skimming through the notebook.

“I find it rather interesting. With the stories stopped halfway, the reader might be even more intrigued. And the pictures they conjure might even be more interesting than the real endings.” Aoandon thought deeply.

“That’s just what you think. I don’t think he intended to mean it that way,” Maestro sneered.

Jinkougyou smiled and took the notebook. “It’s a shame to admit that I didn’t put much thought in keeping the travel log. I simply write down what’s on my mind. I don’t really like reading it again.”

“Really? So it’s a habit you don’t like? But for what?”

For what? The question brought fragments of Jinkougyou’s past back to him. As someone who had recorded thousands of incense recipes, he was never into recording trivial matters in life. After all, he’d lived a carefree life during his childhood. Back then, he thought life was splendid and that it would take him forever to note down every little thing. So he decided to live life to the fullest. The habit of recording every single little detail in a notebook and preserving it belonged to another man.

A gentle breeze swept by the pavilion and crinkled the incense cloud, covering the look on Jinkougyou’s face. He smiled and said helplessly, “Someone in my family liked doing things like this. I guess I do it for him. But I’m trying to teach him a lesson since he’s being lazy. He even asked me to travel and see the world in his stead. I want him to understand that there are things he should do in person rather than asking his brother to help him. So I’m keeping the endings of the stories to myself until he follows the path I took and finds me again.”

“Again? So this family member of yours has been trying to find you?”

Jinkougyou smiled. “Yes. He’s been following my every step.”

It’s just, he’s quite shy and rarely shows up. But… whenever Jinkougyou himself reached a dead end, he would always show up before him almost immediately, as if he had never left at all.

Mountain creeks disappeared into the dusk as trees raged with the wind, creating ripples that spread across the forest until they met the treacherous cliff. There was no chittering of insects or screeching of birds in the picturesque scene. The silence was dead enough to make one feel alarmed.

Hikaru adjusted his breathing in front of the trees s he looked into the vast forest, feeling annoyingly helpless.A few days ago, his brother claimed to have found a blessed land in the mountain and had guaranteed that there were rare incense materials there, but he had disclosed no further information after leaving the mountain. It took Hikaru hours of searching until he finally found the place in Mt. Onkou. But now, standing in front of the last trace of the clue, h was lost again.

Hikaru pondered for a moment and remembered something. He rummaged through his pouch and took out an incense before lighting it in the censer. After a short while, rather than pale smoke, crystalline butterflies flew out from his censer. They hovered in the air for a moment before heading towards a direction, as if something was guiding them. Hikaru felt relieved and followed the butterflies as he carefully entered the thick forest. After walking for a while, he stopped before a cliff. He worriedly looked down, below the cliff’s edge, and saw a pair of eyes that looked just like his. Hiro, on the other hand, looked surprisingly happy as he waved his hands to call his brother while hanging on the vines in the air, ignoring the dangerous situation he was in.

“I was just thinking. If none of my ways work out and no one comes to save me, I could just settle down here. They’d probably be dead anyway after a dozen years or so, after all.”

Hiro said as he playfully swung on the vines. He had managed to acquire the rare incense ingredient after going through several troubles, which included disturbing the beasts in the forest. He was cornered on the cliff when he was hit with a burst of inspiration. He decided to hide under the cliff, and this helped him escape from the beasts. He just didn’t expect to see Hikaru when his plan failed to proceed any further. He briefly wondered how Hikaru managed to find him, but as the butterflies formed from fragrant smoke landed on his hand and he smelled the familiar scent, he immediately understood.

“So that’s what you’ve been using…” Hiro lamented.

Egotia Trackers. He had casually distributed his Egotia to his family members. He thought his cold-hearted brother had already thrown it away. And yet, Hikaru had kept it well and even used it at the most crucial moment. He looked at Hikaru’s poker face and laughed.

“What are you laughing at?” Seeing that Hiro didn’t seem to be bothered, Hikaru was infuriated. “So now what? How am I going to get you, the prey, out of there?”

“Nothing. Just pull me up and I’ll show you the way home.”

Hiro reached out his hand as if nothing had happened. The calm look on his face suggested he was quite confident. Hikaru grabbed his hand with doubt. Hiro suddenly burst into laughter. He threw some other ingredients into his censer, and, with a loud bang, his censer exploded. A cluster of smoke rose up and carried them both into the sky like a firecracker.

“Haha, what do you think about my new incense?”

The young man’s loud laughter echoed in the air as they flew across the forest like a wind. Just when Hikaru thought he could relax, Hiro continued.

“But… since I’ve sacrificed my censer, I’m counting on you for a safe landing.”

The smoke cloud turned thin as he finished his words and was unable to carry the weight of the two brothers, who then started falling from the sky. It would be hard to imagine a more dramatic moment. Before Hikaru had time to blame his irresponsible and planless brother, he lit up the incense in his censer in a moment of truth. Smoke turned into a giant butterfly and caught them like a giant hand. It gently danced its way in the air and softly landed at the foot of Mt. Onkou.

“That was close. But, seriously, you make a fine butterfly with your incense. How about…”

Hiro was still curiously checking the butterflies after they landed. Before he could further compliment them, he turned around and saw the back of his brother who was walking coldly away from him.

It was late in the afternoon. Everyone in the pavilion was speechless after hearing Jinkougyou’s story. After a long moment, someone broke the ice.

“What a deep bond you have with your brother… And I really miss the kind of spirit your brother has.”

It was unclear if this was meant to compliment Hikaru’s lenience, or Jinkougyou;s limited mind when he was young, but Jinkougyou was nonchalant regardless. He gently smiled as he lifted the lif on the censer and put some new incense in it. Then he silently slipped the travel log into the censer.

<sup>[1]</sup>升腾而起的新香如同轻薄的云雾笼罩四方，顺着这水墨淋漓的山水流淌而下，恍然望去，时光就好像回到了曾经的御香山的无数个午后一般。

寻香行难得如少时的时光一般，惬意闲适地靠着亭边，静静地继续回忆着那之后的事。



那一次意外过后，他从父母那得来了一通数落，而那小心眼的弟弟，也自然在自己的笔录中狠狠记了他一笔。

寻也对此不以为然。

少族长觉得，这世上哪有这样的闲人，没事就将自己的过去翻来覆去地翻看，等熙这样一本一本记到长大，只怕一屋都放不下，而到时候积灰的角落就是这些笔录的归处。

只怕到了那时候，他自己看了都想要为自己的小心眼掩面呢。

少年自负凌云志，却不想，世事易移——

他的人生并不算长，那样闲适而又美妙的时光自也短暂。

只是那时候他也并不知晓，最终自己却成了那个，将过去反复回味的“闲人”。

——到而今，春华落尽，满怀萧瑟。

那不起眼的琐碎日常，将会是他在漫长的孤旅中唯一的慰藉。

无边寂静能将人吞噬，唯有将这些回忆翻来覆去的摩挲，才能支撑他的脚步继续往前。

但不知去路的绝望，终究是无法被挣脱的。



远处的香气缥缈而清淡，似乎下一瞬便要消失。

他急切在黑暗中踉跄而行，走入了一片石林之中，脚下崎岖而散乱的石块，剐蹭出了无数淋漓新伤，想要留住他的脚步。

他勉力支撑这自己在崎岖陡峭的山路中走了不知多久，却始终找不到出路。

最终，还是因为疲惫不堪，脚步一错，滚落了下去，坠入了一片漆黑的湖泊之中。

在绝望中，溺水之人无力地挣扎着，意识也渐渐远去，落入无数记忆的碎片之中，在如同潮流的时光之中，裹挟着他在眷恋的记忆之中不断的循环往复。

欢笑、喜悦与宁静充斥着他的心，但生机也愈发微弱，如烧灯续昼一般，逃避着痛苦的未来，勉力营造出一片虚假的繁花似锦。

如果就此沉溺于此，那么，一切的苦痛是否也将离我而去？

就在他怀着这样的念想，将要在黑暗中沉得更深时——

有熟悉的气息袭来，轻柔地包裹住了他，将他混沌的意识骤然从黑暗之中的甜梦中唤醒了。

万花筒般缤纷轮转的时光碎裂开来，露出了其下的真实。

那些欢笑着的人们都安静地躺在黑暗寂静的棺椁之中，御香山晕染的晚霞化作了他们棺椁中堆叠的鲜花，世界沉入一片死寂之中。

他怔然地俯下身，看见了身前的棺椁中熙沉睡的面容，颤抖着地伸出手，却终究无法触及。

生死相隔，又怎么能够轻易跨越呢？

烛泪一滴滴落在无形的屏障之上，却悄无声息地渗入了其中，落在了沉睡之人的脸上，他的身形渐渐化为了无形的香。

梦亦有时尽，神亦有时灭，一缕烟痕无断绝。

缘有未绝，化为蝴蝶。

烟云所化的细蝶如同潮汐一般涌出，一如许多年前的那个傍晚一般，因着亲人的思念而来，牵引承载着他离去，自山穷水尽处脱身而出。

他回头努力想要再看清身后，却只在烟笼雾罩见到一个远去的身影，那身影似乎顿了一顿，还是回过身，向他的方向摆了摆手。

他从这一场大梦中醒来，不知何时已身至湖泊岸边，而衣袖间，还留有余香。

就好似，是熙再一次在他山穷水尽时找到了他。



香炉中逸散出的云雾浅淡了几分，那几位有趣的新友人似乎也已经将话题聊至了各自曾游览过的奇山异水。

就在这时，神香炉微动，盖沿掀开了一点缝隙，气势汹汹地将他那册游记吐出。

愣是让人看出了几分愤愤来。

寻香行将那册子翻开，却见到了几个凌乱爪印，重重地踩在了前文上，而后面的全新纸张上则用香料印出了几个大字。

“统统不知所谓！”

他轻叹一口气，慢条斯理的翻到新的一页，将自己新酝酿的故事写上，然后不知悔改地继续断在了在关键的时刻。

“没有办法啊，哥哥我啊，就是这么个风格。但是没有关系，只要习惯了，便不会觉得看着难受了。”

要是不高兴，那便亲自来找他吧。

毕竟如今万事皆平，他也再不会轻易走到山穷水尽时了，那又要到哪里去等人呢？

就如这游记一样，凡过往，皆为序章。如今的族长大人如了大家的意，开启了新的旅程，也书写了全新的旅途正篇开端，但至于后续嘛——

后续，就等那位心焦气躁的读者自己来写上吧。

This was the Talisman Pass for June. The focus of this event was Kokorogari Kijo Momiji.

The limited time rewards was the Frosty Maple skin, Frosty Maple's Dance illustration, 心狩 illustration and 寒鸦渡影·霜叶 frame.


**枫月宴**

月明星稀的夜，庭院中挂上了几盏纸灯，虽然看上去添了几分明亮，但是依然难以消解空气中弥漫的烦闷气息。


绕过门庭，敞开的屋内更是明亮，晴明与黑晴明坐于屋内，他们皆收到了红叶的邀请，来到红叶林中。


如今六道的局势炙烤着所有人的心，每个人虽然表面平静，没有说出口，但是内心都在为未来的局势而有所担忧。


源博雅观察着红叶林，“红叶林自从上次的危机过后，现在看上去倒是还好，还没受到更多的影响。”


“这些都只是暂时的，今日所看到的，明日可就不一定还是这样了。”黑晴明冷冷地说，他看上去对宴会并没有多少兴趣，“都设好了庭院宴席，却唯独不见邀请者？”


“如若无事，那我便不再多留了。”


“不急。”


晴明看上去倒是从容不迫，毕竟他也不是第一次被一封信邀请到这红叶林里了。


“就算是在最紧张的时候，也需要缓和的时间。”


“既然有人专门设宴，酒已斟上，菜已上齐，不妨就在这月夜里，饮一杯吧。”


黑晴明没有言语，但也没有拒绝，坐回了座位上，若有所思地举起酒杯。


但就在这个瞬间，一阵急风吹来，庭院和屋内的灯都被风吹灭，浓雾四起，寒意骤然而生。


小白从坐垫上跳起，警惕地盯着那股弥散而来的雾气。席间众人皆提防之姿。


雾气中忽然传来银铃般的笑声。


伴随着笑声，枫叶簌簌作响，舒卷的雾气之间，一个美丽的身影渐渐显现。


“既然人都来齐了，那我们的宴会就从现在开始吧！”


“晴明大人……们？”


心狩鬼女红叶从雾气中走出，姿态优雅，笑靥如花。


一如这春日之枫，诡异至极，却有着摄人心脾的美，让人能够忍受弥漫的寒意，驻足观赏。


**弄枫舞**

红叶走入房间，大天狗依然是防御的姿态。


“没事，先坐下吧。”


黑晴明示意大天狗坐下，大天狗虽然有所顾虑，但还是坐回了席间。


晴明依旧是不慌不忙，“在宴会开始之前，可以问问你邀请我们二人前来，所为何事吗？”


“许久没见面，当真一开口就问我这个问题吗？我倒是更期待私密的话题呢。”红叶说道。


“想要与你们见面，不知道这个理由是否可以呢？”


红叶走到了晴明的身旁，俯身在晴明耳旁。


“自从上一次离别过后，实在又让我等待了太久呢。”


“你最好能够快一些，把这些恼人的事情解决以后，好来继续举行我们的婚礼啊。”


晴明抬扇，自然地隔开他与红叶的距离，红叶也借势回到宴席中央。


“既然是宴会，为什么这么沉闷呢？”


红叶扫了眼众人。


“不如，我来增添点乐趣吧。”


红叶挥挥手，庭院中的许多枫叶被风吹进屋内。枫叶互相纠缠着，渐渐变出了人形，有乐师，有舞者。顷刻之间，音乐流淌席间。


红叶抬起手臂，在空中划出一道优美的曲线。跟随着音乐，红叶踏着乐声起舞，周围变化出的舞者也都跟随红叶共同起舞。


红叶的长发随着舞步飞扬，犹如一条黑色的丝绸在空中飘荡，看着她的舞姿，席间的人不知不觉便沉浸到了舞曲之中。


“最近……”


细碎的舞步之间，一声弦响，似乎是在提醒人们不要过于沉醉。


“我听到了一个令我有些疑惑的故事。”


“不知晴明大人们可否为我解答一下呢？”


**枫之问**

“在许久之前，有这么一个女孩，她降生于父亲的祈愿中，可是父亲的祈愿不为她，而是为了他自己。”


“她事事回应父亲的期望，以父亲的命令为准则，于是忘记了自己，失去了自己。”


“即使顺承父亲的命令，竭尽全力地追逐，父亲也从不爱她。”


“她的一生都为家族的利益，为父亲的野心。”


“她不是女儿，不是她自己，她是父亲的囚徒俘虏，是他攀升的台阶。”


“女儿试图做出一点改变……立刻就被父亲抛弃，她受尽磨难，在痛苦中孤寂地走向死亡。”


“如今……女儿重获新生，她所做的第一件事情，就是去寻找那个抛弃她的父亲复仇。”


“她去从前的府邸，但是那里已经破败成废墟；她去父亲梦寐以求的宫殿，但他所渴求的位置上坐着其他人；她去宗族的墓地，却也寻不到她所熟悉的那个名字。”


“她找啊找，你知道最后她在哪里找到的吗？”


“她在饲养牲畜的棚子里找到了他。”


“那个曾经高高在上、不可一世，连一点多余的目光都不愿意分予她的父亲。”


“那个需要她时刻战战兢兢，确保不会惹怒的父亲，此刻正和牲畜挤在一起。”


“肮脏，腐烂，臭不可闻。”


“他已经老得不成样子，花白的头发干枯不已，一片片地绞在一起。”


“而他的脸，就犹如群山走势，布满沟壑，双眼勉强地在那些沟壑间睁开两条缝，双眼蒙着一层浑浊的膜，犹如死鱼的眼珠。”


“有男人走近，提着一个大木桶，他看见她的父亲，便叫骂了一声：今天又来这里蹭吃的！”


“在男人的叫骂声中，她的父亲有所怯懦，走出了棚子，但在男人倒完饲料离开以后，他就毫不犹豫地踢开其他牲畜，趴在食槽上进食。”


“女儿叫了他的名字。”


“多么伟大的时刻啊，父女的久别重逢！”


“可是父亲只是抬头看了女儿一眼，他的眼神中看不出悲，看不出喜，看不出一分一毫的波动，只有深渊一般的麻木。”


“他没有任何的反应，便低头接着与牲畜们抢食去了。”


“女儿曾经想过无数种重逢的方式，可是所有的设想都在这一刻显得荒谬可笑。”



**雪凝枫**

红叶将画满红枫的扇子打开，轻摇扇面，无数栩栩如生的枫叶从画中落下。


四散的枫叶中，红叶优雅地踏叶而舞，每一个舞步都踩在枫叶之上。


“你们说，在这个故事里，想要复仇的女儿和忘掉所有的父亲，后面应该如何呢？”


风吹动烛火，明灭映照进黑晴明的眼眸，他掐灭了这恼人的烛火。


“……罪孽，不会因为时间而消失。”


“因果，终将会应验到每个人身上。”


“女儿的因不解，就会引来新的果。心结当然要由自己解开。”


“既然早已定下复仇的目标，那就应该一以贯之。”


“多余的东西，碍眼的东西……”黑晴明的指尖在酒杯边缘摩挲，他看向晴明。


“当然是彻底消失的好。”


“如果只是消失还不够，那就……惩罚到够为止！”


红叶笑意盈盈，舞至黑晴明身边，带来一阵微风，她的指尖拂过他的鬓发。


“这个回答……倒是不错。”


黑晴明哼笑一声。


“我倒是更好奇，我的阳面，光明伟大的晴明，你的回答是什么？”黑晴明向晴明发问。


晴明并不急着回答，只是将折扇放在手中，随着乐曲的节拍，轻轻拍打。


“有时候……若是过分耽于仇恨或复仇。”


“是不是也算跨入另一个囚笼？”晴明回应黑晴明的目光。


“身体虽是自由，心却又被自己禁锢。”


黑晴明饮下一杯，不为所动。


“谁在笼中，谁在笼外，不到最后一刻便不会见分晓。”黑晴明说道。


晴明摇扇，不置可否。


“在我看来……”


“新生的女儿已经自由，困住她的囚笼已经破碎。”


“所以于她而言，没有往前或是往后。”


“而是——任何方向都可以前进。”


“旁人的建议已不重要，因为这一切都应该由女儿来决定。”


“这些决定所带来后果也都只会由女儿自己来承担。”


“空虚？饶恕？痛苦？后悔？这些统统都落不到我们身上，自然不应该由我们的一言两语来左右。”


“选择的苦痛便是自由的代价，这是无法避免的。”


晴明饶有深意地看向红叶。


“呵呵……”


红叶突然笑了起来。


“哈哈哈哈哈哈哈哈！”


红叶的笑声越来越大，舞姿也越发恣肆，脚边的枫叶竟被踩踏的风不断带起。


随着节奏的加快和音乐的逐渐升高，乐声越来越激烈，乐曲行进到高潮。


红叶的舞步也跟随着音乐越发激烈，她的舞姿犹如流水一般流畅，优美却又富有张力。


每一次手臂的舞动都恰到好处，犹如羽毛般轻盈，又犹如绷紧的弓弦那般有力。


她时而轻柔、时而狂野、时而慵懒，所迸发出的能量比刚才更加令人震撼。


雪女看得入了神，竟然忍不住摊开手掌，雪花冰晶从她的掌心中飞出，一朵朵雪花在红叶的身边旋转绽放，跟随着她一起舞动。


雪花落在红叶的衣摆上，在枫叶上凝结成了点点冰晶。


雪染枫叶，霜结寒鸦！


枫叶在霜雪间更显红艳，美丽夺目，热烈地释放着生命力。


在乐曲与舞蹈皆完美地融合到至高的一刻，红叶轻盈地步上晴明的桌子。


她弯下腰，以优美的舞姿为晴明斟上一碗酒。


“我好像更偏爱您……的答案一点呢。”


晴明抬扇，稳稳接住递来的酒碗，坦然迎上红叶那毫不顾忌的眼神。


源博雅连忙捂住了小白的眼睛。


“这个暂时就别看了吧！”


不明就里的小白挣扎着，“这里这么多人，为什么就不准小白看啊！”


一曲毕，红叶落回地面。


周围的乐师、舞者们也皆瞬间变回枫叶。


方才那热烈的舞曲还留有余韵，众人不由自主地为这落幕感到可惜。


“真是一个完美的宴会啊。”红叶向众人行谢幕之礼。


“那我现在，也该去为这个故事划上一个句号了。”


“哦对了，如果想要听故事的结局，下一次，就由晴明大人你们来找我吧。”


红叶走至门口，月色洒在她还凝结着霜晶的衣服上，月光流华，冰晶耀目，一如她此刻的笑容。


“从现在开始，我就已经开始期待我们的下一次见面了。”


骨鸦展开翅膀，红叶向后倒去。无数枫叶之中，红叶已然乘风而起。


她的身影逐渐远去，最后的话语在风中飘来。


“我给我的心爱之人……留了枫叶信物，请好好珍藏哦……”


黑晴明摊开手，一枚枫叶出现在他的手上，叶脉中游走着奇异的光芒。


而晴明亦觉得手中隐隐异样，他伸出手，一片同样的枫叶在他的手上。


黑白晴明对视一眼，晴明低头看向手中的枫叶，嘴角不由得勾起一抹笑意。


This was the Talisman Pass for July. The focus of the event was Oniou Shuten Douji.

The limited time rewards for the July Talisman Pass were the Chromatic Champion skin, Chromatic Feast illustration, 畅日闲弈 illustration and 曜霞烈饮·酣跃 frame.

This was the Talisman Pass for August. The focus of the event was Gyourou Arakawa no Aruji
