The limited time rewards for the August Talisman Pass were the Kraken Conquerer skin, Sea Surfer illustration, 战意奔腾 illustration and 浪川英豪·鲲龙 frame.


**巡航**


“实在无趣，这片海域，竟遇不上一位势均力敌的对手。”


荒川之主乘着龙船遨游天际。海妖们得知他的到来，全都隐去身形，销声匿迹。


毕竟荒川之主的威名早在四海传开，传闻他性格暴烈，骁勇善战，四海统领皆败于其手。




“救命，救命啊！”


俯瞰海面，一位渔民正抱着一根粗大的枯木，在水中沉浮。




“你没事吧。”龙船落到海面，荒川之主将水中的渔民捞了上来，登上龙船的渔民惊魂未定，瑟瑟发抖。




荒川之主看向渔民，面带疑惑的询问道：


“何事令你如此慌张？”




“快！快往北面逃！那只大水怪要来了，再不逃我咱们也会被它吞进肚子里的！”


渔民拉住荒川之主的衣袖，语无伦次地说道。




经过仔细盘问，荒川之主才明白了事情的原委，原来渔民住在东南边的一座海岛上，一周前，几只魁梧的凶恶海妖上了岛，要求他们村每年献出五位童男童女作为供品，村民表面迎合，背地却设下陷阱赶走了海妖，结果没过几天便遭到了报复。


一只百丈高的巨型水怪从海面中升起，张开血盆大口，吞下了岛屿和岛上的部分居民。眼前的渔民跑得及时，顺利登上一艘小船。然而水怪脚蹼拍起的浪花将他的小船打翻，不得已他只能抱着一根浮木，漂流到此。




听完渔民的描述，荒川之主起了兴趣。


“百丈的巨型水怪倒是少见，看来有必要去见识一番。”


“你，你不要命了么？”


听闻渔民的发问，荒川之主微笑着拔出腰间的利刃，利刃在阳光的反射下发出凌冽寒光。




“吾乃统领荒川的一方英豪，闲来无事，正好去找那水怪松松筋骨。”



**孤岛**

告别了渔民，荒川之主操纵着飞龙之船，朝着东南方航行，海面之上风平浪静，并未有任何异常，仅能看见一座满是火山的孤岛。


“去岛上问问吧，说不定有岛民见过那只巨型水怪。”


飞龙船降落在了海岛之上，岛上除了一座座巨型火山，植被稀疏，四处张望也看不到任何人烟。


走了一段路，荒川之主发现了一只魁梧的海妖，于是走上前发起了询问：


“喂，你有见过附近海域的水怪么？”


“吵吵嚷嚷的，是活腻了么！”


脸上留着刀疤的海妖骂骂咧咧地转过身，当看清眼前之人后，它二话没说，扔下三叉戟便拔腿就逃。




孤岛的另一边，几只海妖正围在一起在商量着什么。


“喂，那边交涉的怎样，岛屿上的家伙们妥协了没？”


“哼，那是当然，他们本想不从。我一把捏爆了村长的脑袋，剩下的家伙就乖乖就范了，下个月开始便有新鲜的肉可以吃了。”


“还是大哥你厉害，以后咱们就不愁担心饿肚子了。”


一路沙石飞溅，长着刀疤脸的海妖气喘吁吁地奔走而来，它一把拉住同伴的手腕，语无伦次的说道：


“快！快！我们赶紧离开这片海域，就当从没来过。”


“哈？为啥？”


“你还记得我们是为何来到这里的么？”


“当然记得，不就是因为老家被毁了，无处可去，才不得不来这片海域上谋生的么。”


“那个……那个把咱老巢端了的家伙也……也……！”


刀疤脸海妖颤抖地指向远处的那个灰白色的身影。


“久别多日了。”


荒川之主扛着那把锋利的海国作走向他们。



**征战**

为首的海妖慌忙吹响了号角，随后便一个接一个地跳入海水之中。荒川之主追到岸边，此时地面开始剧烈震动起来。整座海岛升腾而起，岛上的火山也一并喷发，大量透明的粘液从火山口涌出，被粘液沾染的绿植，瞬间便化为了灰烬。海水翻腾，一只长着细长脖颈的鬼首从海面探出，用赤红的双瞳瞪着岸上的荒川之主。


只见，荒川之主越过粘液，敏捷地跳上飞龙之船，操控飞龙腾空而起。




回到高空俯瞰海面，原来方才降落的地方并非海岛，而是这只巨型水怪的背甲，而上面重峦叠嶂的火山其实是长在背甲上的藤壶怪。




水怪张开血喷大口，朝空中翱翔的飞龙射出数发火球，荒川之主操控龙船敏捷地躲过射来的火球，然而落入水中的火球却引发漫天海雾，趁着荒川之主被海雾干扰视线之时，一束火球穿过海雾，成功击中了龙船，巨浪过后，海面再次回归平静，空中早已没有飞龙的身影。




“自不量力的家伙，竟敢惊扰吾之清梦。”


巨型水怪摇晃着脑袋，从背甲中伸出柔软的脚蹼，滑动着，准备再次潜回水中。




就在此时，一阵剧痛传来，海面被瞬间染红。




两刀下去，被斩断双蹼的水怪痛苦地在水中扑腾哀嚎。它抬起头，看向声音传来的方向，方才被火球击中的荒川之主，正毫发无伤的浮在空中。




“你这家伙究竟是……何方神圣。”




话音未落，刀光一瞬，赤红的喷泉飞涌而出，鬼首掉落，坠入海面激起千层浪花。荒川之主将手中的利刃入鞘，夕阳的光辉照在他的肩上，如披黄金战甲。




“吾乃荒川之主，只是途径此处罢了。”



**启程**

海风呼啸，这场暴雨持续了很久，金鱼姬的船只被海上的巨浪打翻，迷失在了风暴之中，当她再次醒来时，发现自己正躺在一处沙滩之上。




一股鲜甜的香气袭来，金鱼姬立马坐起身，不远处的烤架上，正放着几根金黄流汁的烤玉米，冒着热气，滋滋作响。




旅行多日，身上的干粮早已吃完，再加上这难得一遇的海上飓风，饥肠辘辘的金鱼姬已好几天没有进食。


顾不上别的，金鱼姬抓起烤架上的玉米，便狼吞虎咽地啃了起来。




“有妖怪！有偷吃的妖怪！”


一声呼喊传来，没过多久，一群拿着火把，手持农具的渔民便围了上来。




看到渔民们，金鱼姬立马慌乱地解释道：


“抱歉……我，我只是太饿了，并无敌意。我的船在暴风雨中被打翻了，之后便被海水冲到了这里，等我想办法回到荒川，定会为各位准备谢礼。”


“你，来自荒川？”


一位拄着拐杖的老村长从人群中走了出来。




金鱼姬木讷的点了点头。




“哈哈哈！远到而来就是客，几串玉米算什么，来，尝尝我们村里地道的南瓜米饼。”老村长一脸热情地将金鱼姬迎入村子。




夜间，海滩上燃着篝火，新鲜的鱼获摆满了烤架，酒足饭饱的村民们正围着篝火起舞。




金鱼姬拿出海图，向村长询问该岛的位置所在，村长只是摇了摇头笑道：


“我也不知这座岛如今位于何处，此岛是一座漂流之岛，会顺着洋流漂移，因而总能收获满满的鱼获。再加上孤岛远离战火，岛上的大家生活安逸富足，我想世间的神仙岛也不过如此。”




“漂流的岛屿？这还是第一次听说。” 金鱼姬瞪大了眼睛感叹道。




老村长拂过下巴的胡子笑着说道：


“这座岛的前身其实是一只巨型水怪的背甲，那水怪被一位来自荒川的大妖斩杀，背甲风吹日晒，最终被绿植覆盖，我们本是失去家园的海上流民，在命运的指引下来到了这处宝地。”


“若你感兴趣，可去村中心看看，那里有着我们为那位荒川英雄所立的石碑。”




篝火晚宴结束，金鱼姬独自一人来到了村中的石碑前，石壁上刻一只身形庞大的水妖和一位与之搏斗的大妖，金鱼姬弯身，将怀中野花放在了碑前。




数周后，热情的岛民协助金鱼姬建造了一艘金鱼宝船，登上宝船的金鱼姬与岸上的村民挥手告别。




看向一望无际的大海，金鱼姬不再迷茫，她将踏遍憧憬之人年轻时所走过的足迹，体会他所历经的一切。




“荒川的英雄啊，期待我们在下一个目的地，再次相会。”


日暖风和，扬帆起航。

Izanami slumbered in the Aether Sea. Dark tides rose and fell. In the infinite darkness, the goddess could still see the moment she’d created her first world. The goddess of creation sat up in the waves, the prayers from the old world echoing in her ears.

A little blue butterfly flew towards her. Tired, it fluttered down and landed on Izanami’s finger.

———

“O Izanami, mother of the world, of everything, the ever-merciful, please gaze upon your believers and hear our woes…”

I kneeled on the ground, praying to our goddess of creation. My forehead touched the back of my hands as I smelled the putrid smell of the mud. I pressed my hands together then clapped twice. When I stood up, I saw a scarlet sky. The Jade Columns had wilted. Dried leaves fell and were devoured by the swelling tides.

I had repeated the same prayer countless times. We were born as children of Izanami. We knew her name before we learned of our own. We first learned to pray to her before we learned to speak. However, at this moment, I could not keep praying.

We lived in the world she had created, and that world was coming to an end. I heard the whispering of my friends. They faced the blood-red sky and cried at the sight of a wilting world.

“O Mother, please grant us shelter. Bless this world with life. Bless it so that it may live on…”

But no matter how much they begged, the end was coming. We were doomed. Our world was doomed.

The Jade Columns supported the land. Should trees wilt, tides would wash everything away. But the trees had wilted long ago. All of us, even the omnipotent Izanami, could only delay the inevitable for a little while.

When we prayed to her for the first time, Mother shed tears of blood, so the tide receded momentarily. When we prayed again, Mother scattered her flesh, so we learned the necessity of making sacrifices. And so, the old, the weak, the defeated, and those who drew the losing lot, boarded the reed boats. The water carried these sacrifices away, so that we, the lucky ones, could live to see another day on the last bit of remaining world.

We continued to pray to Mother... My forehead touched the back of my hands as I smelled the scent of tears. I pushed my head closer to the mud as I thought to myself, “Who would board the reed boats this time? Would it be my friends, or would it be me? There’s an end to every one. There’s an end to every tree. And there’s an end to the world. We can keep on praying and making sacrifices to deny the inevitable doom, but what is the point of such fruitless struggle?”

O Izanami, mother of the world, of everything, the ever-merciful, please hear my prayer...

Let the world end. Just as our life began in your embrace, grant us an end most just—returning to the embrace of destruction.

Izanami drifted in the Aether Sea, surrounded by the ruins of countless, faded worlds. These worlds had been born in the palm of her hands, the very same hands which destroyed them. The prayers and cries of the denizens of those worlds turned into butterflies as they flew towards Izanami. Their number grew so large that Izanami was unable to count them all. She wasn't willing to hear them, nor was she willing to care.

Izanami gazed across the rising and falling of the dark tide, and stared at the other god of creation who had been born alongside her, her destined nemesis. She smiled. A newborn butterfly landed on her hair, wailing silently with sorrow.

"O Izanami, mother of death and oblivion, the ever-ruthless, please gaze upon your believers and hear our confessions..."

I kneeled on the ground, praying to our goddess of destruction. Thorns pierced my body as I smelled the stench of blood and rot. If this scarlet flood washed over this land, I would receive solace from the endless pain; it would be as if Mother had at long last absolved my original sin.

We lived in the world she'd created, but the world was born of Mother's pain. Such was the nature of our original sins. We were destined for a path towards destruction, just like the thousands of worlds she had created. Before the final day comes, we must repent with our suffering; we must bring doom to others, so we can please Mother, just for one moment. We must never stop.

And so, we reveled as our world plunged into a never-ending war. We must burn down statues of Father, cut off the heads of heretics, seek out enemies outside the shrines, and expose the traitors within. We kill what's worth killing, and destroy what must be destroyed. If all our enemies are reduced to a mass of red, we must shape new sacrifices from our "selves".

I must continue, until the thorns of repentance have pieced my feet, my hands, and my eyes, as I press them against the ground. From the smell of blood, I learned the truth about destruction. It's the annihilation of our enemies, of us, of this world. Through blood, I saw Mother gazing upon us behind the scarlet sky, smiling.

O Izanami, mother of death and oblivion, the ever-ruthless, please hear my confession...

Does such destruction bring you joy? Will my sins be forgiven? We offered ourselves along with our world with war. When everything ends, will you embrace me and deliver me to the blissful shore?

Izanami fell towards the endless Aether Sea below. The only thing she could see was the last world she had tried to interfere with, the one she'd failed to harvest. Before Izanami was devoured completely, a butterfly appeared and landed on her hand. It was the last butterfly she ever saw.

Heian-Kyo was almost flooded, but it was saved by a golden barrier. People knelt behind the barrier all and prayed to all kinds of gods.

I had never believed in gods. When I saw the all-consuming tsunami crashing against the barrier, I didn't know what to pray...

No, I did. I wanted to go into that dark tide. I kneeled on the ground. Compared to this, my own pain felt like nothing. In the fear of my impending doom, I was met with a thrilling jubilation.

All pain stems from "difference". There is difference between humans and spirits. There is difference between the poor and the rich, the talented and the inept, the abled and the challenged, and even the faith in gods... And so conflict arose between humans and spirits. Good and evil, as well as crime and punishment, created a great divide between people and gave rise to endless wars. Some were sacrificed. Some were saved, but only for a moment.

The Sun Goddess created everything, and divided the world into light and dark, creating a difference. Some were born to live in the sun, while others were destined to live in shadow. Only in destruction can everything be equal.

I knelt on the ground and offered my prayer to the rising Aether Sea in earnest.

O Aether, the beginning and end of everything, please hear my plea...

Please descend upon this realm. I had no choice in my own creation. Please devour me, the sun, the shadows, and all the difference and injustice in this world. Just as everything was born from nothing, let everything return to the void, the only justice.

Seimei put down his painting brush. The long scroll was draped across the stone table, adorned by falling cherry blossoms. The Courtyard was the same as usual. Kusa and Kagura sat side by side beneath the eaves, watching Hakuro and Hiromasa compete in an archery match. Kyuumei Neko was yawning on the roof. The Paper Dolls were pretending to be hard at work, sweeping the endlessly falling leaves. Everything were the same as it had always had been in the countless days that passed.

Seimei looked down at the painting he had just finished. Before he could see clearly, his huge sleeve knocked the inkstone over. Ink poured out like a dark tide and washed over the entire the scroll.

"How could you be so careless, Mister Seimei?" Yao Bikuni glanced at the blob of ink and lamented, " What a shame. Who was it that you painted, Mister Seimei?"

"It doesn't matter," Seimei smiled, "it was a story that has long since ended."

The sky was clear. The sun shone through the leaves, illuminating the stone table. A few butterflies came from nowhere and landed on the ruined painting, drinking in the ink like licking fluid off dead flesh.

That story has come to an end, but it can never receive its resolution. Life goes on. As long as there is difference. the despair that ushers one forwards destruction will persist alongside the hope of life, like shadows to light.

Recently, a man had been loitering under the famous cherry tree in the mountain. Always dressed elegantly in expensive clothes, he would gaze at the cherry blossoms in a relaxed manner, with a hint of unreadable expression. Most of the time, he just stoo there staring, allowing the fallen petals to sprinkle on his shoulders, as if he would never be tired of this view.

His somewhat strange behavior could be justified, however, because this cherry tree always had the most beautiful bloom. People believe that it’s blessed by gods with love. It’s only natural for someone who truly loves cherry blossoms to be obsessed with it. Over time, the man had become a familiar sight for those who came to see the cherry blossoms. No one ever talked to him, however, because he didn’t seem very approachable.

Until one day, he briefly looked away from the flowers when he was distracted by two men talking under the tree. They were an old monk and a warrior. Unlike the usual benevolent monks, this monk looked rather sinister; unlinke the usual majestic warriors, this warrior looked disheveled and spiritless.

“You look very upset, Master,” the old monk said, “It’s a pity, for the blossoms are so attractive. Why bother coming every day if you are not in the mood to enjoy this view?”

“I’m sorry that you’re bothered by my presence,” the warrior said, “But I’m here for a reason. Please forgive me.”

The monk leaned in a little, showing his interest to know more. That would have been rude, prying into the affairs of others, especially considering that they were almost strangers. However, maybe because the warrior was feeling lonely or maybe because the listener is a monk, the warrior started to share his story.

“My wife and I were very close, but she passed away in the past winter. I loved her so much, and I still miss her every day. I just can’t let go. We used to come here for the cherry blossoms every spring, but now, she’s no longer with me, so here I am, all alone… These blossoms remind me of her, and it hurts… Alas, I would do anything to get her back.”

The warrior looked even more defeated after that. The monk, however, didn’t show any compassion at all as most would. He merely stayed silent for a while before smiling mysteriously.

“You are in luck,” the monk said, “Do as I say and come back here when the waxing crescent rises the next time. I can bring your wife back to you.”

It was a very lovely night. The cherry blossoms fell gently in the night breeze. Under the clear light of the crescent moon, they looked like a blanket of white snow in winter. This scene should have mesmerized any cherry blossom enthusiast. Unfortunately, at this hour of the silent night, not all were in the mood to enjoy it.

The warrior came as promised, not only still disheveled, but also anxious and perplexed. The tree he and his late wife used to admire the most could not interest him anymore.

“I did exactly what you asked me to,” the warrior said, “Please bring my wife back for me as promised.”

The monk nodded slightly, “Of course. You may not know who I am, but anyone who knows me knows that I always keep my promise.”

The monk turned his gaze to the box the warrior was holding with a tense grip, as if it contained something with unbearable weight.

The warrior asked, “Many say that the dead cannot be revived. Many have tried, but to no avail. Yet your task for me was... easy. Why?

The monk turned the prayer beads in his hand and softly chanted a Buddhist mantra.

“Easy?” He seemed to be asking himself, “Well, you are a warrior, so taking another person’s life is indeed easy for you, I guess. But the man you killed will never come back to life. How dare you assume that his life is worth less than your wife’s?”

The warrior didn’t know what to say. With a light wave of the monk’s shriveled hand, the seemingly heavy box was opened. The night wind suddenly rose, carrying with it the shimmering dust in the box, which then dispersed and disappeared with the dancing cherry blossom petals. Strangely enough, the cherry tree appeared even more beautiful and enchanting.

“This is interesting… Very interesting… To get the love of your life back, you didn’t hesitate to commit a sin as heavy as hers,” said the monk.

The warrior looked as if he was about to scream in shock. The monk looked straight into the warrior’s eyes, quietly folding his hands, as his pupils slowly turned into vertical slits, like those of a snake.

“Please be patient, Master,” the monk said, “Your weight has been placed on one side of the scales.”

“Though the best of spring has already passed, the late cherry blossoms are just as beautiful. Let’s enjoy them together, shall we?”

The journey back from the Underworld was a long one. Day after day, the warrior waited for his late wife to return, and he finally saw her in the night when the last batch of late cherry blossoms bloomed.

The moonlight crept in through the window, casting the shadow of a woman outside onto the paper screen, outlining her beautiful silhouette. In her final days, illness had reduced her to a frail skeleton, but now she appeared as healthy and curvaceous as when they first met.

The woman knocked on the door gently, which hadn’t been heard in this household for so long. The warrior, overjoyed, jumped to his feet and rushed to the door, eager to see her, the one he had been pining for. However, as he reached the door, he stopped in his steps and felt a sudden and subtle uneasiness rising in his heart.

Since she’d been to the Underworld, was she still a human? Or had she become a ghost? If she were a ghost now, how would they be getting along in the future? He wanted to see her, but was afraid to do so at the same time.

The woman sensed his hesitation and decided to speak up.

“My dear husband, why the hesitation?”

Her voice almost brought tears to the warrior’s eyes. It was the very same voice of his wife that he was so familiar with.

“We promised we would to go see the cherry blossoms together every spring. And here I am. Please let me back in.”

But… something just didn’t feel right, thought the warrior. Yet he couldn’t put a finger on it. He cracked the door open just slightly to peek outside, wanting to confirm it was indeed his wife. Just then, he realized why it felt so strange. It used to be him on the other side, gently knocking and calling out. His sleepy wife would come to open the door for him and then extinguish the lantern she had lit for him under the eaves. The warrior was suddenly caught off guard when he met the gaze of an eye staring back at him from the other side.

“You didn’t leave the lantern on for me…” she said.

Horrified by that grim tone, the warrior smashed the door close. Realizing that he was not letting her in, the woman’s warm tone became harsh.

“You wanted me back so much that you did not hesitate to kill. Yet you didn’t leave the lantern on for me, and now you dare not even see me, shutting me out. Clearly, you’ve let go of your love for me. Then why did you even bring me back from the Underworld? Why are you doing this to me? This is all your fault!”

On the other side of the thin door panel, the curvaceous beauty had vanished, replaced with a decaying skeleton that wailed in sorrow, “Your heavy, relentless obsession has trapped me here, keeping me from attaining peace…”

The voice, once familiar, now sounded less like his wife and more like the sinister hiss of a venomous snake.

The late cherry blossoms remained beautiful under the clear moonlight. The cherry blossom season had reached its end, yet the most beautiful and magnificent tree never seemed to be withering. It’s believed to be blessed by gods with love.

The man admiring the cherry blossoms under the tree gently shook his head and said, “The beauty of cherry blossoms lies in their cycle of blooming and falling. They flourish only for a fleeting moment, then fall delicately after reaching their peak. Whether it’s love or life, it’s more interesting to let things follow their natural course.”

Nearby, the warrior, face pale, fell on his knees in front of the old monk and pleaded, “I want to take back my wish. Please allow my wife to return to the Underworld and let her have peace.”

“I told you, Master,” said the monk, “The person you killed would never have the chance to come back to life either. How can a wish be taken back so easily? Besides, you promised to give everything, and what I asked of you was nothing to you. It’s just too late to back out now.”

The warrior tried to straighten up but felt an almost unbearable weight pressing down on his spine. He knew that it was his wife, who he had brought back to this world. For the rest of his life, he would have to carry her, along with the sin he had committed for her.

The mysterious man, actually the Serpent Lord, admiring the cherry blossoms now revealed his true form, looking down upon the suffering soul. He could see that the old monk was in fact a naga that he knew all too well, and the weight weighing down the warrior was merely an illusion created by the naga.

He hissed something, and the naga followed suit. The warrior then heard his wife on his back speak again. He looked horrified and hopeless, his eyes resembling lifeless black voids. In endless despair, he pulled out the sword on his waist. A sudden gust of wind swept through, stripping the cherry blossom tree of its blooms in an instant. The loud rustling covered the dull sound of the blade piercing flesh. The naga slithered back to the side of the Serpent Lord. Now that the warrior had atoned for his sins, the ‘wife’ he had called back from the Underworld could finally find peace and leave.

“Enjoyed yourself? That was quite a show.”

The warrior’s body transformed into a pile of cherry blossoms and collapsed like a snow mound in the wind. The naga picked up a petal in his mouth and presented it to his master. The Serpent Lord examined it for a while and then gently blew it away.

“Humans sin for love, and die because they lived. Like the cherry blossoms, they bloom and fall within a short time, leaving no traces. This world, though far from perfect, finds charm in its flaws.”

Perhaps, the Serpent Lord mused whimsically, he could make this world even more interesting.

It was a bleak autumn night. Moonlight shone through sparse clouds, casting a dappled glow over the forest. The light shifted with the evening breeze but couldn't dispel the chill and eeriness hanging in the air.

Alone, I prowled the woods, hunting down any demons and monsters that might stir up trouble here. For a long while, the only sound accompanying me was the faint chirping of insects. Suddenly, a frantic rustling broke the silence. I turned toward it and soon spotted a villager stumbling through the brush, fleeing as though his life depended on it. The moment he saw the Minamoto emblem on my robe and the long blade at my side, he collapsed, relief washing over his face.

"Please, Master... save me!" he stammered, voice shaking. "He won't stop”

"What happened?" I asked.

"A savage wolf spirit's been terrorizing the mountain. He... he killed my brother."

The villager went on to describe a creature unlike any ordinary wolf-its movements swift and ghostly, eyes blazing with an eerie blue flame, and a crescent moon shining from its brow. His brother had gone up the mountain to hunt, only to fall prey to the beast. And when he'd gone back to collect his brother's remains, the wolf spirit had turned its sights on him.

"I couldn't just leave my brother's body out there to be torn apart by beasts or battered by the rain. But that cursed monster... it's worse than any wild animal, prowling next to the body, awaiting its next victim," his voice laced with anger.

He'd been running ever since, as if trapped in these woods by the wolf spirit's spells. By nightfall, he still hadn't found his way out, and the monster had kept close, almost as if it found some twisted pleasure in the chase.

Once I knew the direction of his village, I guided him through the forest.

Whether the wolf spirit feared my presence or simply lost interest, I sensed no demonic aura trailing us. nor did we face anv further spells blocking our chase.

Once I knew the direction of his village, I guided him through the forest.

Whether the wolf spirit feared my presence or simply lost interest, I sensed no demonic aura trailing us, nor did we face any further spells blocking our path.

As we parted, the villager looked at me, his voice earnest. "I beg you, Master... Please rid us of that creature before it harms anyone else."

His fear of the monster seemed to overshadow his grief over his brother's death. But considering how he'd just barely escaped the monster's clutches, I could hardly blame him. With a nod, I tightened my grip on my blade.


I returned to the forest and began tracking the wolf spirit's path. The villager's panicked footprints were easy to follow, his steps haphazard as he'd fled. Though he'd felt like he'd been running for half a day, the tracks told a different story—he'd merely been running in circles, ensnared by the monster's spells. This pattern of footsteps pointed me straight to the source, and soon enough, I caught sight of the wolf spirit.

It looked exactly as the villager had described: a twisted, unnatural form

 with ghostly blue flames flickering in its eye sockets and a crescent moon glowing on its forehead. There it prowled, circling a small clearing. Beside it lay a broken skeleton, likely the villager's unfortunate brother, who'd met his end in this same cursed spot.

The clouds parted suddenly, and moonlight spilled across the clearing like a flood of silver. The wolf spirit lifted its head and howled toward the sky.

There was such sorrow in that howl, a deep and resonant grief, and to my own surprise, I felt it strike a chord within me. Why would a creature that terrorized others bear such sorrow? And why did its anguish stir something in my heart?

Before I could make sense of it, the wolf spirit sensed my presence. In an instant, its grief faded, replaced by a feral rage as it turned and charged.

Gone was the mournful creature, and in its place was the savage, bloodthirsty monster I'd come to slay.

With a single shout, I drew my blade and met its assault. The monster's claws lashed out, slicing across my forehead, and warm blood trickled down the side of my face. But I paid it no mind. In the midst of battle, my eyes,

or distraction. To the villagers, this monster must have been a terrifying scourge, but to me, it was hardly a challenge. Among the countless fiends I'd faced, this one was unremarkable. Before long, my blade pierced its body. But instead of collapsing, it let out one final, harrowing howl, and to my surprise, its form dissolved into a thin wisp of smoke, scattering into the night.


An uneasy feeling weighed on me as I stepped closer to examine the remains lying in the woods. The corpse was in a pitiful state, barely recognizable, with one hand outstretched in a desperate, frozen grasp—as if reaching for help that would never come. But something strange caught my eye: the wounds bore no trace of demon force. This meant it wasn't the wolf spirit that had killed him, but some ordinary wild beast of the forest. Yet if there was no connection, why was the wolf spirit lingering here? And if it truly existed, why would the villager lie about how his brother died?

Puzzled but lacking any leads, I turned away.

That night, the wolf spirit returned—in my dreams. The scene was the same: the bamboo grove, the silver moonlight, its sorrowful howl piercing the night. Once again, I felt that overwhelming sadness I couldn't quite understand.

Just then, my master appeared beside me. The wolf spirit's eyes, burning with eerie blue flames, flickered briefly before locking onto him. In an instant, it lunged, claws brimming with dark energy aimed straight at him.

And for reasons beyond me, my master made no move to dodge. Just as the wolf spirit was about to strike, I stepped forward without a second thought, placing myself between them.

Pain tore through my chest as the demon force passed through me, snapping me awake

me awake. Outside my window, the waxing moon shone, unaware of what had happened. It had only been a senseless nightmare. I would never allow myself to be so powerless, much less my master. But still, I couldn't shake the thought: perhaps there was meaning behind my choice in that dream.

Even now, the wolf spirit's raspy, twisted voice lingered in my ears.

"You and he share no bond, yet you risk your life to protect him. And I, his own blood, was betrayed over a handful of silver…


 The next morning, I made my way to the village to let the villager know his brother's remains could finally be laid to rest.

"The wolf spirit's been dealt with," I told him. "You don't have to fear him anymore."

Relief washed over his face, and he smiled wide. "Oh, thank you, Master! I can't tell you how grateful I am!"

I studied him for a long moment before speaking again. "I understand you lost your mother young, and your father just recently passed. It was just you and your brother left to look out for each other, wasn't it?"

He blinked, clearly surprised by the shift in conversation, a slight stiffness creeping into his expression. "Why are you bringing this up now? Yes, just as I told you yesterday..."

I continued, "Your brother's body bears no trace of demon force. He wasn't killed by the wolf spirit. And from the position of his outstretched hand, it seems he was reaching for help. Someone was with him as he was dying."

I left the words hanging there, choosing not to press further. The truth was clear. They had gone hunting together, but when a pack of wolves attacked, his brother had drawn them off, giving him a chance to escape and find help. Instead, greed for his brother's inheritance had crept in, and he'd abandoned him there, leaving him to die alone. Now, he'd returned to retrieve his brother's remains, hoping to claim his inheritance once and for all. He hadn't foreseen that his brother's final moments, consumed with betrayal, would give rise to a vengeful spirit—a spirit that now haunted him, demanding retribution.

But while his actions might be deplorable, there was no law that could hold him accountable. And though the wolf spirit's reason was nearly spent, it was too unstable, too dangerous to be left unchecked. I could repel the monster, yet I had no power to punish this man who had sinned. Words would only complicate matters, so I took one long, hard look at him, then turned and left without uttering another word.

Later that evening, back at the Minamoto Mansion, I recounted the events to my master, leaving out the nightmares. He regarded me, easily reading the weight in my gaze. With a steady hand, he tapped the bandage that now covered the cut on my forehead, his tone calm but resolute.

"You are our clan's sharpest, most prized blade. Do not dull yourself in unnecessary battles, nor let your heart waver for unworthy causes."

Yet even as he spoke, I could still hear the wolf spirit's final howl, lingering in my memory. It was gone from this world, yet the weight of it pressed on my mind.

A voice deep within urged me that this was far from "unnecessary." If I truly sought to forge an unshakeable heart, it would be up to me to cut through the haze and find clarity on my own terms.

This was the Talisman Pass for January. The focus of the event was Taishakuten.

The limited rewards for the January Talisman Pass were the Royal Audience skin, Theatrical Dream illustration, 七宝莲心 illustration and Lotus Stage frame.


This is the third Ascension Celebration since I ascended to the throne.

Three years is as insignificant and silent amount of time as a single feather falling from a bird. But it is real, none the less.

The post-war recovery of the Celestial Realm has been going well. The Celestial Realm and the Demon Domain have been connected. The imperial palace is prosperous. And the villages on the boarders have become much more lively. Things are developing as I had hoped. I've grown accustomed to the noisy crowds and the numerous celebrations, big and small.

Inside the King's Hall, the priest read the list of gifts and senders as usual.

And I was listening as I always did. However, when he mentioned that specific play from another race, its melody suddenly came to mind despite my hazy recollections of the past...

I had seen a bit of that play when I was still just a kid. I was surprised by how much the feelings of the characters resonated with me. I felt as if I had

been transported to the world of strife in the play, that I was living another life, witnessing the sorrows and joys of the mortal realm and the fragility of life. By the time the curtain came down, my face was full of tears.

As a kid, I thought the pain in the play was as painful as it could get in this world. I hadn't a clue that it was an omen of the things to come. Later, I witnessed the thousand-year war between the Celestials and demons. On the frontlines, I saw how tumultuous the world could become.

The erupting cheers of the citizens pulled me back to reality. I was in my habitual robe embroidered with golden lotus, riding on my six-tusked white

•elephant. Below me were the lively citizens of the imperial capital, a living picture of prosperity.

I placed a delicate piece of Heian-Kyo pastry into my mouth, as I calmly watched the celebration held for the Trayastrimsa God. It was time. The gong sounded. The drums grew loud. A woman in red gently pushed the white curtain to the side and walked onto the stage on tip-toes to start the prelude to this grand show. She was young and beautiful, her moves were graceful, but I heard a dissonance from the shards of her consciousness in her voice.


The voice of the actor was soft but filled with sadness. She spoke of her deep love for opera, of how much she related to the characters, then lamented how she couldn't bring herself to watch the play's sad ending. On the stage, she sang a song of dreams and spring's return. But in her heart, she anguished about the cruelty of fate.

"The drifting lovers should have got together in the end... How could they cut ties with each other just because others criticized them."

"They made it through wars, through life and death, through all those hardships..."

"How could such deep bonds be severed and discarded... so easily?"

The dissonance grew loud. Her years of rage had nowhere to go and morphed her inner demon into a miasma. The overwhelming miasma howled as it rushed towards the pillars supporting the stage. The lotus I released prevented the stage from collapsing before the pillars fell, but the citizens were already fleeing in panic. The celebration had descended into chaos.

Soma stepped in front of me, to shield me from the evacuating crowd and prevent the miasma from making its next move. The plinth with the pastries had collapsed, the delicate pastries scattered over the ground and stomped into pieces. To prevent the situation from escalating, I calmed the white elephant I was riding and commanded Biruri to lead the Celestial soldiers guarding the venue, and evacuate the people from both the eastern and western exits, to minimize the risk of a stampede.

Suddenly, I heard a faint call among the chaotic confluence of thoughts. It was a mother who'd lost her child. I jumped down from the white elephant and took shelter under it. With the white elephant and Soma's protection, I pressed my palm to the ground. A golden lotus quickly bloomed in the square. In that instant, my mind was flooded by the fears of the crowd. In them, I searched for the cries of the child.

"Mom..."

Found it. I extended my right hand and made a path paved with lotus flowers. A loaded shelf was about to fall onto the child who was huddled in a shop, but I got there first. I pulled the child into my arms.

"Everything's fine now."

Everything was fine, as I had absorbed the fears of the crowd. I stood up and looked towards the inner demon, who was perplexed by the situation. I raised my finger gently and created a realm, drawing both mine and the actor's consciousness inside.


It was a void filled with dazzling white.

I carefully recalled the play which had been staged and recreated what I saw on this pure white ground. With a mere thought, a river, a bridge, and several buildings sprang into view. The scene seemed familiar to me. I felt like I had done the exact same thing with someone important.

The inner demon was weeping, having realized the extent of the disaster it had just caused. Upon seeing the scene, it quickly attached itself to the characters, performing like a professional actor.

She was playing out the perfect ending she'd always wanted. I watched silently. She made the main couple, who were supposed to be living separately as hermits, reunite and cry. She let them live to old age, surrounded by their children and their children's children. She brought the war-filled world to an age of pace, prosperity, and happiness. But then she stopped. I asked her why. She just looked at me for a while before lowering her gaze and shaking her head.

"I've been performing this play for ten years." She said after a long while.

"The seats were full every time. The audience cried, laughed, raged, and cursed... This is the first time I'm met with silence." She raised her head to look at me again, "is it really so bad to have a happy ending?"

She was desperate for an answer, but I remained silent. She fixed her gaze on me, attempting to decipher my expression.

"Is my answer really that important?"

"Of course!"

"What's more important to you? The feelings of the audience, who are outside the play, or the feelings of the characters?"

"They're equally important, of course."

I raised my hand and recreated the chaos surrounding the stage for her to see.

"It would appear that the audience is of no importance to you." I dispelled the illusion and smiled,

"if the characters

are more important to you, then

my answer won't matter.

"But if the characters are truly more important to me... from whom should I seek answers?"

"How about yourself? You are the one who plays the characters and also the one who writes the story. Take a look at yourself from a moment ago. Would you say you were the characters or the audience?"

..."

"In other words, do you like the ending just now?"

"I do." She answered without hesitation, "I know all things are fickle and most people don't get what they want, but I still prefer happy endings. I want everyone to live. I want lovers to end up together, hard work to be rewarded, sincerity to be met with sincerity, and parted friends to reunite.

The ending may be more realistic when there's regret, but there are too many regrets in this world, and that's why a happy ending is what truly reflects people's wishes. My master once said that a ticket creates a play, and a play creates a dream."

"And that is plenty."

"But this is just my dream. Once it ends, I'll have to follow the original script again. That's why I still want your answer. You're the only one who saw my show, after all..." She turned to look at me, "a sad ending or a happy ending, which one would you choose?"

"To me, the story doesn't end with the play. The ending of the play is only a temporary pledge from the author."

I looked towards the hut with a smoking chimney on the distant hill, where the main character of the story would end up living, then said, "If I were the author, I would pave the way for my main character and give him the ending that I considered to be the best. If I were the character, I would have faith in the author and believe that he did everything he could to give me the best ending."

After hearing these words, the inner demon nodded and spoke in the same tone I used,

"and that is plenty."

Then, the inner demon slowly disintegrated into miasma, which then dissipated and turned into golden dust, scattering across the illusory set.

I was left looking at this little world I had created alone. I wanted to invite that person to take in this magnificent view with me, but I couldn't remember their face or name, so I had to give up on that thought.


I placed a delicate piece of Heian-Kyo pastry into my mouth, riding on my six-tusked white elephant. As thousands of my citizens cheered for the magnificent play, I applauded with them. As the celebration was coming to an end, I waved my hand and concealed this illusion into a mirror, revealing the real square.

As suggested by the demons, this square was constructed by the place where the Demon Domain connected with the Celestial Realm. A cold wind blew past the border, with decorative ribbons fluttering the air. The banners of Unsullied Heaven ruffled in the wind.

The actor on stage was shocked to discover that the chaotic scene of people fleeing was just an illusion. The crowd was shocked to discover that the wonderful show on the stage was never real. It turned out that everyone was a character in the play all along. The crowd then began to cheer once again, praising the king's name in their songs.

On the square, treasures were displayed from all over the world. White doves flew, accompanied by songs of joy. Thus ended the third Ascension Celebration since I had taken the throne.

However, there was one thing that caught my attention. When I recreated the stage from my memory, I found that... It was my father and older brother who brought me to the Ascension Celebration that day. They held me as we sat at the center of the celebration. But when I recreated the stage, my mind only directed me to build it as it would appear from the back. No wonder I subconsciously thought that I had only glimpsed this play when I was young.

As I pondered, a feather fell from a passing white dove. It quietly drifted across the air and landed on m palm. I looked up, only to see a colored ribbon rising higher and higher into the air. I didn't know where it would go. Nor did I know in whose palm it would land. I simply watched as it disappeared from my sight.

This was the Talisman Pass event for February. The focus of the event was Shoufuku Zashiki Warashi.

The rewards for the February Talisman Pass were the Chirping Kite skin, Afternoon Kite-flying illustration, Start Flying illustration, and Chirping Sparrows icon frame.


In the sound of the fluttering wings of spring birds, I traveled through winding roads and returned to that familiar Courtyard in Heian-Kyo. Here, I spent many a spring, found my resolve when I was lost, met many who I now consider my friends and family, and even gained the strength to venture out on my own to distribute good fortune. Perhaps this is what "home" is about.

"Home." Every time I close my eyes, the view of the Courtyard emerges clearly in my mind. The sakura petals are falling slowly. Master Seimei is writing something under the trees. The paper Dolls are sweeping the fallen petals together into a small pile,which resembles the orbs that burns on a spinning pinwheel. Everything is so pretty and peaceful...

"Alright, everyone's here! Ta-da! Here's your question, 'What is fortune?'" "Hehe, let the selection begin!" "Who will it be? Which one of us is best suited to play the role of 'Zashiki' and bring fortune to the Courtyard?" I was startled, hearing my own name out of the blue.

I peeked into the Courtyard and saw something entirely different from the serenity l had expected. In fact, it was the complete opposite. What a lively sight.

"Did you not know?" Kagura said to me, "This is the latest celebration that's popular in the mortal realm."

Why would there be a celebration where people dress up like me...?

"Lately, people have been saying that they seem to have more good fortune than before." Kagura said, "they've been saying, 'I'm sure it's because Zashiki visited our home. So people in a village by a river held a celebration, and it all began from there."

"They would ask the children to pick out the child that resembles Zashiki the most to dress like her. It's to pray for good fortune and give children blessings of good health."

Tilting her head, Kagura looked at me with her clear and smiling yes and said quietly, "It's also to show you their gratitude, Zashiki."

I rubbed my face. It was burning hot. I understood why every spirit in the Courtyard was so eager for this... Everyone was hoping for some good fortune and wanted to work hard to help others, like me.

Yamausagi said, "Hehe. I think good fortune is having endless candy. Also! It's having peaceful dreams protected by Ninja Master!"

Mouba said, "That's not it. Good fortune is Bitey, who runs fast and never looks back!"

Shiro said, "It's meeting all the people that are important to me in the Underworld."

Doujo said, "It's Master Seimei! It's staying by his side!"

The discussion turned heated while I struggled to pick the winner as I agreed with all of them... Then, a cold wind blew across the Courtyard.

It was supposed to be spring, a season of warmth and blooming flowers, but the cold wind took me back to winter. Yuki appeared under the sakura tree out of nowhere, glancing at us with a cold gaze.

"I thought it was Master Seimei who summoned me here," Yuki sneered, "but it turned out to be bunch of kids playing house."

"We were not playing!" Yamausagi replied with discontent, "we were having an important discussion about 'fortune'!"

Kusa raised her dandelion above her head and said, "but you're a kid too. Will you join our 'Zashiki' competition?"

Yuki pressed his lips together.

"Fortune?" He said, "that's merely happenstance. Chasing after fortune is like chasing after snow, which will eventually melt. It's too insignificant and won't be able to turn things around when you need it. It's useless...”

His spoke with great apathy, but he stopped when his eyes met mine, seemingly embarrassed.

When he left, the cold wind seemed to have been driven away completely by the warm spring sun.

However, the chill didn't leave me. So, I stood up and went to find its source.

When I found Yuki, he was standing on the cliff by the Courtyard and looking down at the town. The spring sun was bright. Everything was filled with vitality. A flock of birds flew across the sky and landed in a lush patch of green.

"A long time ago, I was taken in by an old couple, who thought l'd be their

'Zashiki." Said Yuki, "but unlike you, I can't bring fortune. I coudin't help them. I couldn't save them."

"So... I shouldn't be joining in on such a celebration. I'm sorry. What I hate is myself. I didn't mean to blame you and fortune."

Before I came to the Courtyard, I was also lost and full of hatred like him.

The fortune I can bring may be insignificant when stacked against the forces of "fate."

It can melt ice and snow, so that people may reunite after being long apart. It can offer solace to lonely children. It can remind people of their old ambition.

It can give people courage. It can create lucky encounters...

But it can't change fate. It couldn't save my mother.

But, but...

As insignificant "fortune" may be, the moments it can create may already be enough.

Isn't it "fortune" like these that put a smile on people's face?

And wasn't it because of "fortune" that I was able to met my mother?

"You must have wanted to bring them happiness and good luck," I said to Yuki, "that's why you always think of them. You can remember their smile, right? The reason they could smile was none other than you."

Yuki frowned, still puzzled.

"It took me a long time and a lot of help from others to get it," I said, "which is why I want to keep spreading more and more good fortune to more and more places. The moments it can create are insignificant, but they are real.

Just thinking of happy moments they had in the past is enough to help struggling people hang on a little bit longer, long enough to see the coming of spring."

"For now, we can start with just Master Seimei's Courtyard... Would you join me?"

Yuki stood silently for a long while, but eventually reached out and held my hand.

When we returned to the Courtyard, I suddenly realized I had forgot something... Ah, I heard Yamausagi's voice...

"Since the judge isn't here to make a decision, and everyone's opinion turned out to be the same, then this is it!" Yamausagi shouted in high spirits, "I hereby announce, the winner of our 'Zashiki' competition is Zashiki!"

Wh-What?

Was that also the work of fortune...?
This was the Talisman Pass for March. The focus of this event is Miketsu

The limited rewards for the March Talisman Pass were the Prosperity Granter skin, Stove Flames frame, Golden Prosperity illustration, and Burning Prosperity illusion.


  The arrival of spring brought an endless stream of worshipers to the shrine. I listened carefully to their heartfelt wishes, most of which were no different from those of the previous year. However, a young girl caught my attention.

In the early morning, as the bright, refreshing air gently unfolded, she would arrive at the shrine just before sunrise, beads of sweat glistening on her forehead. She came every day, quietly making the same request, and then hurriedly leave.

"Lady Miketsu, please make the persimmon tree in Stillwater bear fruit again.”

Stillwater, a mountain hamlet nestled in the northern hills, was far from the shrine. Yet, strangely, she didn't seem to be heading in that direction after making her wish. What could bind her so deeply to a solitary tree in a distant village?

My curiosity led me to follow her. She walked quickly, and soon we arrived at a busy restaurant in the heart of Heian-Kyo. The girl went straight to the kitchen to light the fire. But as she turned, she noticed my presence in the restaurant.

"Good morning. Can I help you... wait, Lady Miketsu?!"

Surprised to see me, she was visibly flustered, but the demands of her work left her little time to dwell on my unexpected visit.

"Hello," I replied with a smile. "I'll have a bowl of your signature steamed rice, please.

I sat in the restaurant and watched as she single-handedly stoked the fire, prepared the ingredients and cooked the rice, all the while greeting a steady stream of customers. Soon she brought out a fresh bowl of the restaurant's signature steamed rice and placed it in front of me with a hint of anticipation in her eyes.

I sat in the restaurant and watched as she single-handedly stoked the fire, prepared the ingredients and cooked the rice, all the while greeting a steady stream of customers. Soon she brought out a fresh bowl of the restaurant's signature steamed rice and placed it in front of me with a hint of anticipation in her eyes.

The aroma of freshly cooked rice wafted toward me. Without hesitation, l polished off the entire bowl.

"Delicious," I said, putting down my chopsticks. "Such fragrant and tasty rice would bring joy to anyone's heart."


The lunch rush was over, and after seeing off a few lingering customers, the girl extinguished the cooking fire.

"I've seen you every day lately, always in a hurry. Now I know it's because you've been busy running this little restaurant. A wish you've kept even in such a busy life must have a special story behind it, right?"

She lowered her head and smiled shyly, then began to tell the origin of her wish—a story about persimmons.

The girl had once been an orphan, wandering and begging for food. At the time, all she wanted was a single hot meal. Eventually, she found herself in the barren land opposite Stillwater. Too weak to venture into the village to beg, she took shelter one cold night in an abandoned, dilapidated hut.

Outside the hut stood a persimmon tree, its branches heavy with ripe fruit.

Under the starry sky, the tree and its hanging persimmons framed the half-collapsed roof.

"When you're cold and starving, you start to see illusions. I looked up and thought the persimmons were holiday lanterns.”

A gust of wind swayed the "lanterns" and one fell, landing on her head. It was late fall and the persimmons were covered with a layer of frost. To the girl, it looked like a layer of sugar.

"Maybe it was good luck that the persimmon brought. The very next day I met the kind soul who saved my life."

"No need to thank me, the savior said at the time. The grains and vegetables of this world are gifts from Lady Miketsu, meant for all to share."

After expressing her gratitude to the kind old woman, the girl set off alone to the city to learn the art of cooking.

"After my mentor died, I took over this restaurant. Later, I heard about a disaster that hit Stillwater and left the land unusually barren. Many things changed..." "Thanks to Lady Miketsu's blessing, the land eventually regained its vitality over time," she added. "When I visited the village to check on my savior, I passed by the barren land again and saw that the old persimmon tree had withered, never to bear fruit again...

She pointed to a painting on the wall of the restaurant. It showed the persimmon tree, drawn from memory—a symbol of the hope that had sustained her all these years.

"For me, the frosted persimmon then remains the best delicacy in this world."


 As evening approached, the story came to an end and it was almost time for the restaurant to reopen for dinner.

"Lady Miketsu, just the fact that you listened to my wish is enough to make me happy."

Her busy yet reliable figure was outlined in golden tones by the flickering firelight, warming the hearts of all who saw her.

I rose and left the restaurant, travelling to the land opposite Stillwater.

There stood the withered persimmon tree, its branches still reaching for the sky as if gazing across the river. Under the moonlight, its shadow stretched long and thin, silently resting on the surface of the river. It stood there like a forgotten old friend, quietly watching over the land.

Gently, I ran my fingers over its weathered bark, feeling the marks of time.

The warmth of my palm followed the veins of the tree, flowing gently.

Golden light began to seep from the trunk, slowly spreading to the tips of the branches. Brilliant "fruits" began to sprout-persimmons glowing with a fiery orange hue, like lanterns held aloft in the night.

The "lanterns" rose with the wind and drifted across the river toward the village. From afar it was a brilliant sight, a cascade of shimmering light.

Eventually, the glowing fruits settled gently on the eaves of every house in Stillwater, hanging there and reflecting on the surface of the water, like a scattering of stars. Wisps of cooking smoke wafted from inside the houses.

The aroma of burning hearths mingled with the scent of warm rice, creating a sense of comfort and happiness for all who inhaled it.

In ancient times, it was the heat of campfires that protected people from misfortune and allowed them to cook delicious food. Over time, these fires became the hearths of homes, surrounded by fertile fields and filled with the joy of harvest and the warmth of family. The campfires of the wilderness became the hearths of the home, but what remained unchanged was mankind's enduring hope for a good life and good food. As long as these "lanterns" shine, the hearth fires will never go out.

I looked at the lights on the far shore-the glow of home, the very essence of happiness. May these lanterns and hearth fires be my gift for the coming of spring—a blessing of light, warmth and hope.


 After a spring rain, fresh green buds sprouted from the branches of the b0?

persimmon tree. On another early morning, the girl returned to express her gratitude and brought me a persimmon seed as a gift.

"Lady Miketsu, I hope this seed brings you the same happiness and hope it has brought me."

It was still early spring, and a fine mist of rain fell gently, moistening the cracks in the earth. After some thought I decided to plant the seed near the realm where a little companion of mine once lived. Small fish swam to and fro in the nearby pond. As I watched the ripples in the water, I felt a faint sense of nostalgia.

Back then, when that little one got restless, the water in the pond would® swirl into little eddies. Often, just looking at the water was enough to sense its mood for the day. At those times, the sound of my voice was all that could calm its restlessness.

Remembering the times we spent together, it felt as if they weren't so far away... Perhaps it is these cherished memories, the beauty of the past, that allow hope to endure year after year.

"Don't worry. You just have to grow up, slowly and steadily. Just take your time. This time I won't leave."

Gradually I felt the slight trembling of the earth beneath my feet. The soil cracked open as a tender sprout emerged, struggling against the darkness and chaos, much like that little one in those bygone days.

The afternoon sun bathed the bright green sapling, its delicate leaves swaying gently in the breeze. It seemed to be silently responding to the wishes held deep within my heart. Perhaps by this fall it will have grown into a young tree...

This was the Talisman Pass for April. The focus of the event is Ootengu.

The limited rewards for the April Talisman Pass are the Graceful Trailblazer skin, Floral Flute illustration, Midnight Gale illustration, and Springtime Flute frame.


From the clouds above, the sakura grove by the water appeared like a mist of light pink, dyed by the morning glow.

From the heights of the sky, everything on the ground seemed indistinct. For Ootengu, the only things clear while soaring through the sky were the clouds, the wind, the pristine azure sky separated by the cloud sea, the first light of dawn at the horizon's peak, and the distant land of his ultimate aspirations.

Yet, he still recognized this particular sakura grove on the outskirts of Heian-Kyo.

Mt. Gloom was peaceful now. Perhaps bored with such tranquility, Dark Seimei spoke of the sakura grove with interest.

"The sakura must be in full bloom by now, don't you think?" Dark Seimei said. "The noble academies of Heian-Kyo often hold spring banquets there.

The place will become quite lively... The scene suddenly came to my mind.

Why don't you go take a look for me? Who knows, something interesting might happen."

It was a trivial matter, so there was no reason not to go. Ootengu agreed without much thought. But Dark Seimei called out to him again.

"Your attire does carry the refined elegance of nobles on a spring outing,"

Dark Seimei remarked. "There is one more thing l'd like to ask of you."

It was a trivial matter, so there was no reason not to go. Ootengu agreed without much thought. But Dark Seimei called out to him again.

Your attire does carry the refined elegance of nobles on a spring outing, Dark Seimei remarked. "There is one more thing l'd like to ask of you."

"Years ago, I met a young noble boy at one of those banquets. As is common, young men often speak of their grand ambitions during the spring gatherings-whether to achieve high rank or to compose poems that would be remembered throughout Heian-Kyo. Some dreams might come true, others failed, and some were forgotten entirely. But in the moment they were spoken, they were all rather fascinating.

"However, unlike others, that noble boy's aspirations made me stop and listen. I was intrigued, so I would like you to see if he has achieved his vision? Or... has he forgotten it, or changed completely?"

Ootengu asked, "What was his ambition?"

"He wished for peace and stability for humans, for a world where people no longer suffer, and for all demons to be eradicated."

With a sharp snap, Dark Seimei closed his folding fan. His eyes, obscured by dark strokes of ink, glinted with a fox-like cunning, his face carrying a knowing amusement.

In a quiet voice, Dark Seimei murmured, "A foolhardy wish... for a new world."


Ootengu descended slowly onto the emerald grass, which was strewn with fallen sakura. The delicate petals swirled in the breeze, drifting onto the lacquered red table and settling into the cups of liquor.

It was indeed a beautiful spring day. However, unlike the "lively banquet" Dark Seimei had described, this spring gathering was shrouded in an eerie silence.

Beneath the sakura trees, the nobleman Dark Seimei had once met slumped in a drunken stupor. At the sound of movement, his fingers twitched, and he lifted his head.

His face, withered and lifeless, was utterly out of place in such a picturesque setting.

Not all winged Tengu could reach the Heavenly White. Not all ambitious souls could grasp the power of their ideals.

In essence, they were equally elusive and distant. Ootengu had long pursued hurricanes, yet he had never considered that they could so easily destroy a man.

The noble's eyelids trembled

"It's you..." he muttered. "I remember you."

His pale, lifeless eyes took on a dreamlike haze, as if submerged in a delusion.

"That master said he would come to witness my end. Yes, yes, the time has come. Now I understand why he smiled that day when he heard my ambitions... He had already foreseen this outcome. Ah, how foolish I was! I thought human suffering stemmed from demons... thought that if the Minamoto lords cleansed all the demons... Ha! Only now do I realize!

Suffering itself gives birth to demons! It is not demons who shape agony, so long as humans exist, they will continue to create their own suffering!"

Ootengu stepped forward, sensing something amiss. Now, he understood why this banquet had fallen into such a deathly silence.

The noble murmured, "I never forgot my dream. Ah, I have always pursued it, always... Though that master once warned me that chasing an unattainable justice might lead me astray, might consume me... But I did it! I did it!"

With trembling hands, he reached toward a drifting sakura petal. "Look! I have freed everyone at this banquet from their suffering!"

The moment his fingertips brushed against the petal, his body crumbled like mud—his blind, twisted ideals had transformed him like a devouring hurricane.

The decaying sludge surged toward Ootengu. He seemed to hear a voice hissing at him: "And you? The justice you pursue is just as lofty and unreachable. Will you not lose yourself on that endless path?"


Ootengu remembered this sakura grove.

In his youth, his aspirations for the future were unclear. He had once hoped that, after gaining power, he would grow large enough wings. But in reality, he could only hide his small wings beneath his wide hunting robes, reluctantly making his way toward the young Hiromasa, who was tucked away in the corner of the spring banquet.

"I thought you used the flute to call me for battle," he frowned, "We are just fighting partners. Next time, don't call me to such banquets. I am very busy."

But Hiromasa simply said: "The spring banquet has many sweet snacks."

Hearing the description, young oteng tell silent and no longer hurriea to

leave.

The young Hiromasa continued: "I don't like noble banquets either.

But this time is different. It is said that if one speaks their aspirations at a spring banquet, they will receive a blessing. I want to become stronger, so do you. As your friend, I wish you can achieve your dream."

The young Ootengu understood—this was like a human version of Bright Moon Ceremony.

"Don't you already know?" He looked up toward the heights. The sakura branches clustered together, reaching toward the vast blue sky. "I want to have a pair of great wings and fly to the highest place. Once I get there, I should be able to witness a brand-new world."

"You can do it," Hiromasa said. "I've always felt that you will have wings as grand as those of a crane.

"Why crane?"

"Because I want to bless you with it," Hiromasa replied. "And because a crane can fly both high and far, never losing its way."

Later, Ootengu chose the one he swore to follow. After parting ways with his childhood friend, only to find their paths converging once more, he remained steadfast in the pursuit of his own justice, even if the new order he longed to witness would forever remain beyond his reach.

The Tengu are born with the power to command storms, but storms also lead to blindness and misdirection. A voice asked him: "You fly so high, how could you be sure that you are on the right path? Will you not lose yourself on that endless path of pursuit?"


Ootengu thought: "No, I will not."

The raging winds shattered the filthy sludge. Like a solitary crane, Ootengu soared into the sky.

Wind blades cut through the mire, cleansing it layer by layer, revealing the thriving sakura that had taken root in the spring soil. As he broke through the whirlwind of falling petals, they clung to his wings, carried along by his ascent.

He flew higher and higher, until, when he looked down, all he could see was an endless sea of clouds.

Beyond the firmament, everything on the ground became indistinct. Even the sakura grove by the water was nothing more than a faint pink mist, tinted by the morning glow.

Yet something still caught up to him.

He could still hear the flute.

Somehow, a melody from the earth below reached his ears, carried from afar.

He flew toward his lofty aspiration.

oward his lofty aspiration. ed ever present bu his side He

But the truth that belonged to him remained ever-present by his side. He could see himself fighting alongside Hiromasa on the battlefield. He could hear Tamamonomae's words, which were always accompanied by a wise smile. He remembered each of his comrades-those he had met, those he had yet to meet, and those he would never reunite with. And he remembered every battle they had endured together.

He knew that his "justice" was not only in the Heavenly White, but also within the world below.

Just as in his youth, before he had learned to fly, the first thing he had learned was how to fall—

He descended toward the earth.

Dark Seimei stood within the sakura grove. The guests of the spring banquet had long collapsed, unconscious. Scattered petals covered the ground, as if the swamp had never existed at all.

"You have never lost your path," Dark Seimei said with a smile. "It seems that not everyone, unlike Seimei and myself, chooses to sever their ideals from their very being."

"You don't need to test me," Ootengu replied coldly. "I follow you, not Seimei. But wherever I go, it is always of my own will."

He cast a brief glance back. The banquet remained as it had been—flourishing flowers. birds singing in harmonv. Nothing here was worth attention any longer.

This was not the place where innocent boys had once carved their names.

Nor were the people he should remember here.

Yet, Ootengu seemed to catch sight of something. He withdrew his gaze and moved forward

This was the Talisman Pass for May. The focus of the event is Shinjou Hoshiguma Douji.

The limited rewards for the May Talisman Pass are the Spring Splash skin, Angler's Joy illustration, Heroic Courage illustration, and Musa Window frame.


"Hoshiguma Doji! Are you there?

Hoshiguma Doji rubbed his sleepy eyes and saw golden sunlight shining through the window, as well as Ibaraki Doji, who poked his head in to call for Hoshiguma Doji while carefully controlling his fire not to burn the handicrafts scattered around Hoshiguma Doji's cave. With a raise of Ibaraki Doji's hand, the Mt. Oe spirits behind him flooded in and surrounded Hoshiguma Doji with excited chatters.

It wasn't until Ibaraki Doji gave his summary did Hoshiguma Doji understand the situation-everyone pooled together some Jade and reserved him a place at the healing hot spring at Nekogawa Annex, which was famous in the healing hot spring at Nekogawa Annex, which was famous in Heian-Kyo, so that he could have a vacation.

Hoshiguma Doji turned pale. He had heard of the place before, especially its extravagant services and even more extravagant price. Hesitant, he looked at everyone, wanting to decline their exceedingly generous offer, but was met with looks of anticipation and worries—that he might be reluctant to accept.

Yamawaro blinked nervously. Satori fidgeted with her hair. Tanuki rubbed his hat. Karasu Tengu flapped his wings. Ibaraki Doji even held Yamausagi, who was the smallest, and pushed her right into Hoshiguma Doji's face. Lo and behold, Yamausagi's face was bright red from impatience, as if she'd knock Hoshiguma Doji out if he refused and send him to the place by force.

"Hoshiguma Doji, you're always hard at work. It's time for you to relax a little." After all, the payment was already made.

Wine Demon ruffled Hoshiguma Doji's hair with his wings. "Hey, just say yes already. Like they said, they spent a long time saving up and picking the destination. You may think you're doing a good thing to decline the offer because it's so expensive, but you're actually breaking their heart."

The spirits, having planned everything in secret, nodded in agreement like pestles in mortars. Well, since they managed to get this far with the plan, the Demon Boss must have given them his approval. Hoshiguma Doji felt tears welling up and wiped his eyes, saying, "Then your wish is my command."

After all, no one will say no to a vacation.

The news of Hoshiguma Doji going on a break quickly spread through Mt.

Oe.

Sitting in his throne, Shuten Doji waved his hand. Heeding his command, the tanuki spirits at the brewery lined up and delivered Hoshiguma Doji's luggage to him on their heads. Shuten Doji then gestured towards Hoshiguma Doji, telling him not to worry. "For you, this break is long over due."

And so, the spirits walked with Hoshiguma Doji to the path outside of the mountain to see him off. A loud shriek came from the mountains—it was Wine Demon towards them. He turned himself bigger and picked up Hoshiguma Doji by his clothes before taking off into the morning sun. In the sky, Hoshiguma Doji waved his hand excitedly at everyone on the ground,

"Wait for me, and don't cause any trouble—“


Nekogawa Annex is surrounded by mountains and streams, which gives it a different vibe than Mt. Oe. Hoshiguma Doji walked through the door and squinted his eyes in an attempt to avoid the prices written on the board behind the counter, so that he would be spared from knowing how much those guys spend for his sake.

After bathing in a hot spring, Hoshiguma Doji spent some time in the sauna.

And Wine Demon received his own care routine. As they enjoyed the Annex's special kaiseki meal, the young owner revealed more about their services to Hoshiguma Doji with a smile on his face and a cat in his arms: a wild creek behind the mountain was recently developed as a fishing spot, and that it would be a shame to miss out on the experience. He then introduced the Annex's selection of premium fishing gear, which guests could rent or purchase... Hoshiguma Doji's eyes were glued to the shiny fishing rods, but he quickly came to his senses as he realized he couldn't afford them. The cat in the owner's arms raised its tail and pointed towards a stone path behind the Annex, signaling Hoshiguma Doji to follow it.

Under the bright sun, the water was clear enough to make the fish seem like they were floating in the air. Hoshiguma Doji held a musa leaf above his head for shade. He looked into the distance and found that the fish were highly alert—they gathered at the center of the water, several feet away from the bank and out of reach for all but the most experienced anglers.

If the fish won't come to me, then I'll go to the fish. Hoshiguma Doji spread a few fresh musa leaves on a frame fashioned with thick branches and jagged rocks, and made himself a makeshift boat. He rolled up his sleeves and pushed the boat into shallow water. Then, with a push from his bamboo fishing rod, he set sail.

The fish here were all very plump and would pair perfectly with wine when cooked. Hoshiguma Doji was reminded of the gluttonous spirits at Mt. Oe. If he could bring back a basket of fish, marinate them with wine, cover them with salt, pepper, and miso, then grilled them with the Demon Boss's fire until they're sizzling... It would surely make a great meal and please the spirits. If he could do that, then this vacation would certainly be a productive

one.

Suddenly, the boat began to shake, thanks to a particularly fierce undercurrent. It caught Hoshiguma Doji off guard, nearly causing his boat to capsize. Fortunately, it also drew the fish's attention. Hoshiguma Doji thought he could capitalize on this opportunity, so, as he spun with the current, he could capitalize on this opportunity, so, as he spun with the current, he raised his bamboo rod and cast the line with a loud "There!"

Some passing guests saw the scene and whispered to each other, "I heard that fishing enthusiasts would go to the most ridiculous spots as long as there's fish. See that? It's all true."


Wine Demon sat with painting supplies that he somehow acquired and painted the scene of the Mt. Oe spirit's fishing. He asked Hoshiguma Doji about his catches so far.

"It's going well," said Hoshiguma Doji delightedly, "I've caught so many things. Besides fish, that is."

Wine Demon held the paint brush in his mouth and took a close look. A knocked fish basket, a few pine cones, one half of a hand fan, a tassel, a pair of carrot hair clips... and a paralyzed Lantern Soul who fell into the water.

After drying off with the help of the kind Hoshiguma Doji, the Lantern Soul stuck out its tongue and nodded in gratitude before promptly taking off.

Wine Demon laughed. Thanks to the terrain and climate in Mt. Oe, Hoshiguma Doji is less of a fisher and more of a fish griller.

Ripples reflected

sunlight onto the musa leaf boat, giving it a jade-like shine.

Hoshiguma Doji decided to lie down and put away his fishing rod, so that he could enjoy a moment of peace floating down stream.

"What a shame. I planned on catching some fish for everyone back home to go with wine. Now that I can't, I feel like..." Like he was wasting away without a purpose. No wonder Ibaraki Doji said he was born a busy bee and should learn how to take breaks. Hoshiguma Doji wondered that, now that he was awav from Mt. Oe. would Shuten Doii and Ibaraki Doii would run into trouble? Would those feisty spirits get hurt by accident? Would anyone sneak into the brewery to steal a sip of his latest batch? Well, he wouldn't mind anyone stealing a little Ambrosia, but he feared the possibility of anyone opening sealed jars-that would be an express ticket to the bathroom...

As Hoshiguma Doji was spacing out, he subconsciously rubbed the bells on his clothes. Suddenly, a bell bit his finger. Hoshiguma Doii jolted his hand away in pain and heard the familiar sound of arguing from the bell. It was a message left by the others, "Master Hoshiguma Doji, you must bathe in the hot spring for at least ten hours, eat ten boxes of Sakuramochi, and never count how many Jade we spent so you won't lose sleep! We promise to take care of ourselves and won't make things difficult for the boss-Ouch! Who hit me?"

The juvenile chatters of the spirits exploded by Hoshiguma Doji's ear, along with the "noise" of things and spirits getting knocked over, which scared the fish away. Hoshiguma Doii hurriedly covered the bell and saw his own reflection in the water: a person who's frowning and lost in thought. From between his fingers, the bell continued to chatter:

"Anyway, you have to have lots of fun! We won't have peace of mind otherwise!"

Their peace of mind? What about me? Hoshiguma Doji laughed out loud and felt as if a fish had swam gently across his mind.


A spring rain fell like a net woven with silk, covering the green hills in great delight.

Guests gathered in the wooden corridor of the Annex to share tea in the sound of the rain hitting the musa leaves. Hoshiguma Doji sat with a blanket wrapped around him, his hair still damp. Hugging his knees, he fixed his gaze at a certain spot with a tilted head and a smile on his face. Wine Demon poked his bird head out from the blanket and asked, "Still haven't taken it all in yet, have we?"

By the corridor, a half-drenched raincoat was laid flat across the ground. On the raincoat rested a fish over three feet long, which was the topic of the guests' discussions. They had rarely seen a catch as big as this, not even from the elusive anglers from Heian-Kyo. Even the one-eyed cat, who usually napped in the Annex owner's arms, deliberately passed by several times while pretending not to care. Hoshiguma Doji raised his head proudly and said,

"None of us have."

Back at the creek, the message from the bell helped Hoshiguma Doji let loose completely. He crossed his legs and started humming a tune, fully immersed in his leisurely drift down the stream. Seeing that he stopped causing a commotion, the fish that had left previously chose to return. Some grazed his ankle with their silver scales. Some slapped his belt with their tail. Some leaped out of the water and flung drops of water towards his relaxed brows.

Hoshiguma Doji clapped and laughed, saying, "I got nothing when I tried, but got everything after I gave up!" But at this point, he could no longer bear to reel these fish in even if they bit.

Suddenly, a big one came barreling towards him. Due to its high speed, it ended up crashing and stranding itself in Hoshiguma Doji's boat. Hoshiguma Doji stared at this unexpected windfall, stunned. Suddenly, rain fell. He sloppily put on his raincoat, picked up the giant fish, and ran his way back to the Annex.

"I have to run from the rain, so you'll have to be my only catch today. It can't be helped."

—How come fortune favors this guy so much? Wine Demon wondered. He then saw Hoshiguma Doji took out his Wine-making Manual, wet his ink with rainwater, and began scribbling away. 


- What? Did that inspire you to make fish-flavored Ambrosia? That's so out of the left field."

"Tsk." Hoshiguma Doji pushed Wine Demon's wings away. In reality, this manual was more of a journal of his own spontaneous ideas than a record of wine recipes. He used to diligently write down wine making methods he came across, but did nothing with them besides robotically replicating them. Later, he started writing down any new theory and idea he had, which strangely led to him tasting many new Ambrosia flavors.

This time was no exception. He had finally grasped the true meaning behind the "breaks" Ibaraki Doji raved about. Those who're used to taking care of others should learn to accept others' kindness in return. Isn't that a type of kindness itself? This exchange of feelings will deepen the bond between people. However-Hoshiguma Doji laughed. To him, the most important thing in the world, is to be with everyone and lead a happy life.

This is the Talisman Pass for June. The focus of the event was Inari Miketsu.

The limited rewards for the June Talisman Pass are the Golden Waves skin, Ripened Waves illustration, Golden Shade illustration, and Ready Bounty frame.


Outside the window, summer is in full bloom. Sunshine pours down, and the rain is plentiful. Everything is alive and growing, pulsing with energy. The sight always puts Miketsu at ease.

But this year, the heat is especially intense. The afternoon air rolls in thick and heavy, often blowing away her sweet, drowsy mood.

She reaches toward the window, then pauses. Miketsu can't bring herself to shut out the brilliant sunlight or the breeze scented with fresh greenery.

Since her nap isn't going to happen, she sits up and leans by the window, looking out at the lake to the north of the shrine.

The reeds that grew through spring and summer now stretch out in thick clusters. Their blade-like leaves ripple in the wind, looking from afar like waves of cool green.

She watches closely and notices flecks of early golden reeds catching the sun—they shine brightly, and their color almost matches the ornaments in her hair.

"Hmm... if I weave a new reed mat from those, it should feel much cooler." With that thought, Miketsu picks up her basket and heads out, her steps light and cheerful.

The tall reeds cast soft shadows. Fluffy reed flowers drift down in the breeze and settle on the lake, sparkling on the surface like little decorations.
